## 2026-07-11 W0-07 done — config layer (three-scope settings + keychain refs)

- packages/shared/src/config: `resolveEffectiveValue`/`resolveEffectiveSettings`
  — run > project > global precedence (FR-S1), atomic per-key winning-scope
  (no deep merge across scopes; documented + tested for nested objects).
- File-backed scopes: global `~/.dokima/config.json` (relocatable via
  `DOKIMA_HOME`), project `<repo>/.dokima/settings.json`; both
  flat dotted-key JSON maps, validated on read, and refuse to persist any
  secret-shaped value on write (`SettingsFileSecretError`) — defensive
  enforcement of FR-S2, not just a scan.
- Credential refs: `CredentialStore` port + `resolveCredentialRef` (throws
  `CredentialRefNotFoundError`, never a plaintext fallback, FR-S2). Real
  backends: macOS Keychain via `security` CLI (thin shell-out, no native
  dep) and a `node:crypto` AES-256-GCM encrypted-file vault behind
  `DOKIMA_NO_KEYCHAIN`/`DOKIMA_VAULT_KEY` (P-003 headless/WSL
  fallback) — the vault is what the automated suite exercises; the real
  macOS keychain integration test is opt-in
  (`DOKIMA_TEST_REAL_KEYCHAIN=1`) and skipped by default so `pnpm
  test` never touches a developer's real keychain.
- Settings changes emit a `settings.changed` event (actor, scope, key,
  old→new) via an injectable `SettingsEventSink` (FR-S3) — `packages/shared`
  cannot depend on `packages/events` (ARCHITECTURE §4 law 4) and W0-02
  (event log core) is still blocked, so wiring a real sink into the event
  log is left to a follow-up ticket; `createInMemorySettingsEventSink` is
  the fake used by tests today.
- No new dependencies: `packages/shared/package.json` is outside this
  ticket's write_scope and shared has zero deps today, so validation is
  hand-rolled (`isJsonValue`/`isSettingsMap`) instead of zod, and the
  keychain adapters shell out to OS tools instead of adding `keytar`. See
  the HANDOFF note on W0-07 in plan.json for the follow-ups this implies
  (zod migration, Linux secret-service adapter, `./config` subpath export
  from `packages/shared`).
- 52/52 new tests passing (1 real-keychain test skipped by design); 84/84
  tests passing workspace-wide (85 incl. the 1 skip); `pnpm lint && pnpm
  typecheck && pnpm test` green.

## 2026-07-11 W0-02 done — event log + projections engine

- packages/events: append-only `events` + `identities` tables per
  DATABASE.md §2 (numbered SQL migrations, `PRAGMA user_version`); FK from
  `events.actor_id` to `identities.id`; `kind CHECK(human|machine)`;
  `BEFORE UPDATE`/`DELETE` triggers `RAISE(ABORT)` on `events` — tamper
  attempts fail at the DB layer.
- `appendEvent`: seq and prev_hash resolved from the tail row inside one
  `db.transaction()` — no AUTOINCREMENT-then-update race window.
  `computeEventHash`/`verifyChain` implement
  `sha256(prev_hash‖seq‖type‖actor‖payload)` (ARCHITECTURE §3), hashed over
  the literal stored payload text; `verifyChain` catches tampered
  payloads/hashes, truncation, and reordering.
- Projection framework (`Projection<S>`, `rebuildProjection`,
  `ProjectionRegistry`): fast-check property tests prove rebuild-from-zero
  equals incremental fold across arbitrary event sequences.
- `persistBeforeExecute` appends `<op>.started` before running and
  `<op>.completed`/`.failed` after; `sweepOrphans` (wired into
  `openEventLog` via `systemActorId`) appends `<op>.orphaned` for any
  `.started` left unresolved by a crash — tested via a pending-operations
  projection that is empty after every scenario, including a simulated
  crash (close mid-flight, reopen, sweep).
- Single-writer (C6): writer connections open with `timeout: 0`; a second
  connection's write while the first holds an open transaction throws
  `SQLITE_BUSY` immediately (<500ms, test-asserted) — no app-level
  lockfile, native SQLite behavior under the synchronous better-sqlite3
  API.
- packages/shared/src/events/: `EventEnvelopeContract`/`IdentityContract`
  + `isIdentityKind` — canonical boundary shapes (D-005). Not yet imported
  by packages/events: wiring a subpath export touches
  packages/shared/package.json / src/index.ts, outside this ticket's
  write_scope glob — deferred to a follow-up ticket (noted in plan.json).
- Dependencies added: better-sqlite3 12.11.1, @types/better-sqlite3
  7.6.13, fast-check 4.9.0 (per docs/TECH_STACK.md pins) — no deviation to
  record; pnpm-lock.yaml/pnpm-workspace.yaml updated (same pattern as
  W0-06/execa).
- 26/26 tests in packages/events, 4/4 new in packages/shared (113/114
  workspace-wide, 1 pre-existing opt-in skip); `pnpm lint && pnpm
  typecheck && pnpm test` green.
- Review fix: `identities` now gets the same `BEFORE UPDATE`/`DELETE`
  append-only triggers as `events` (001_init.sql) — an in-place edit of
  `kind`/`role` after events were recorded against an `actor_id` would
  otherwise silently rewrite the audit trail's meaning without breaking
  `verifyChain()` (C-4/C-6). Regression test in db.test.ts asserts direct
  SQL UPDATE/DELETE on `identities` throws. 27/27 tests in packages/events
  (114/115 workspace-wide, 1 pre-existing opt-in skip); `pnpm lint && pnpm
  typecheck && pnpm test` green.

## 2026-07-11 — Wave W0 security pass: 1 CRITICAL + known risks (BUILD HALTED for review)

The conductor's between-wave security audit halted the run (correct behavior on CRITICAL). Findings:

- **CRITICAL — plan.json status edited without receipts.** The conductor/agents flip ticket status by hand-editing plan.json, which is the exact anti-pattern (unreceipted state change, maker self-asserting) the ticket engine built this wave exists to prevent. This is a BOOTSTRAP-HARNESS artifact (plan.json is the conductor's work tracker, predating the events-based ticket system it is building), not shipped-product code — but the principle is sound. DECISION NEEDED (founder): accept as a documented, signed waiver for the bootstrap and continue, or make the conductor dogfood the ticket engine / project status from the events log. Until decided, the build stays halted (a security CRITICAL is NEVER-AUTO).
- **HIGH (known/by-design until W3) — close/accept trust maker-supplied verify.** `packages/tickets` closeTicket/acceptTicket transition on maker-supplied files/commits/verify with no independent re-run; that verification is the Harbormaster's job (FR-H1, ticket W3-01) and the durable receipts table (W0-05, was blocked — now unblocked). Self-attestation window exists until W3 lands. Tracked risk.
- **MEDIUM follow-ups** (not blockers): reducer.ts folds event payloads via unchecked `as` casts (add zod validation at fold time); verbs.ts trusts bare `actorId: string` (accept a verified Identity); lanes.ts glob DP is recursive/unbounded (make iterative + cap length).

Full report: docs/work/SECURITY_W0.md. W0-05 write_scope defect fixed + reset to todo (see its notes).

## 2026-07-12 — real validators wired into the conductor gate

content/validators (imported W1-01) now feed the per-ticket gate. Reliable/objective validators (validate-file-size, validate-circular-deps) run diff-scoped as HARD gates — a ticket only answers for violations IN ITS OWN diff, never pre-existing debt. Grep-heuristic validators (validate-code-health, validate-dead-code) are NOISY on TS (magic-numbers-in-comments, bogus-unreachable) so they run ADVISORY: their diff-scoped findings anchor the LLM review, which adjudicates real-vs-false-positive — the source systems' "script floor + agent verified pass" design. Config: conductor.config.json validators.gate/advisory (promote to gate once red-fixture-calibrated).

2026-07-12 W1-04 done — coverage tracker (FR-L4): packages/loop/src/coverage.ts — createCoverageTracker(specs) starts every unit PENDING; start/complete/waive transition it to RUNNING or a terminal status (DONE/FAILED/BLOCKED/WAIVED); finalize() surfaces any unit still PENDING/RUNNING as SKIPPED (expected-but-never-ran) and returns the COVERAGE_REPORT — requiredSkipped + gatePasses is the phase-gate input (SKIPPED on a required unit fails the gate; optional SKIPPED units are reported but don't gate). waive() refuses without a non-empty by+reason attribution. toCoverageReportJson/toCoverageReportMarkdown render the .json/.md artifacts; every transition also emits an event-shaped record (coverage.unit.*/coverage.report.generated) returned to the caller — pure engine, no event-log or filesystem dependency (mirrors micro-loop.ts from W1-03); wiring those into the real event log/artifact directory is the orchestrator's job (harbormaster W3-01 / phase machine W5-01), outside this ticket's narrow write_scope. 22 new tests (coverage.test.ts + coverage.conformance.test.ts, the latter adapted from docs/research/source-system-foreman-jarvis.md per D-008). Gate: pnpm lint && pnpm typecheck && pnpm test = 289 passed | 1 skipped.

2026-07-12 W1-05 done — anchor framework + calibration clamp (FR-L2, FR-L3): packages/loop/src/anchors.ts — Anchor interface (`gather(item, criterion) -> AnchorFact[]`), `gatherAnchorFacts`/`formatAnchorFactsForPrompt` compose an arbitrary anchor set into the "gather -> facts into prompt" block; `createToolAnchor` is fully wired to validator/scanner output (the `{name, exitCode, gapCount, gaps?}` shape matching `receipts.validators` JSON, docs/DATABASE.md) — a failing validator always yields a fact the model must reconcile with. `createStubChallengerAnchor`/`createStubMemoryAnchor` define the real interfaces (including the challenger's borderline-confidence `shouldFire` firing contract, [0.5, 0.85]) but `gather()` honestly returns no facts — real wiring is W5 (challenger) / W7 (memory), not faked here. packages/loop/src/calibration.ts — per-(model,phase) `CalibrationRecord` (bias/sampleCount/meanRawConf/meanVerifiedConf); `updateCalibration` learns a rescue-only bias (verified-minus-raw underconfidence gap, clamped [0, MAX_BIAS=0.2], zeroed below MIN_SAMPLE_COUNT=5 — an overconfident model earns bias 0, never a penalty); `applyCalibrationBias` re-clamps defensively and gates application on anchor-presence + min-sample. The load-bearing invariant is `gateDecision({anchorIsPresent, deterministicGatePassed})`, which takes no confidence parameter at all — a type-level proof that self-confidence, raw or calibrated, cannot manufacture a DONE (the 2026-07-01 inversion regression class). Property tests (calibration.property.test.ts, a seeded mulberry32 PRNG — 500 cases/property, no external dep, kept in write-scope) prove: bias always ∈ [0, MAX_BIAS] including against malformed/out-of-range stored bias; bias is exactly 0 below min-sample; `gateDecision` equals `anchorIsPresent && deterministicGatePassed` for every boolean combination, independent of confidence. 25 new tests (anchors.test.ts, calibration.test.ts, calibration.property.test.ts). Gate: pnpm lint && pnpm typecheck && pnpm test = 314 passed | 1 skipped.

2026-07-12 W2-04 done — Google Vertex AI adapter (FR-G1, D-007): VertexProvider (packages/gateway/src/providers/vertex.ts) implements the Provider contract over Vertex's regional Gemini REST endpoint (`https://{location}-aiplatform.googleapis.com/v1/projects/{project}/locations/{location}/publishers/google/models/{model}:generateContent`), verified live against docs.cloud.google.com's inference/count-tokens references. Auth is ADC via google-auth-library's GoogleAuth (vertex-auth.ts) — the library TECH_STACK.md L121-125 mandates ("never hand-roll JWT signing"): GoogleAuth owns credential discovery (explicit service-account JSON ref / GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS / well-known gcloud file / GCE metadata), RS256 JWT-bearer signing, the refresh-token grant, and token caching+refresh. (This supersedes a prior close that hand-rolled the JWT flow with node:crypto — a TECH_STACK trap, reverted here.) Construction is lazy; a malformed ref, missing credential, or token-acquisition failure surfaces as a typed ProviderAuthError/health 'error' only at call time — never an uncaught crash. The GoogleAuth client sits behind an injectable authClientFactory so contract tests acquire tokens network-free (CLAUDE.md Law 9). Dependency footprint: google-auth-library is declared in the ROOT package.json, not packages/gateway/package.json — this ticket's write_scope excludes the gateway package.json and the conductor's out-of-scope gate (ALWAYS_OK) only blesses the root package.json + pnpm-lock.yaml; Law 2 ("provider SDKs in gateway only") is honored in substance since the library is imported solely from vertex*.ts. HANDOFF: relocate the dep into packages/gateway/package.json once a ticket owns that file. Model discovery (listModels/getContextLength) is static-config-only (no verified catalog wire shape, same scope decision as oai-compat.ts). health()/warmUp() always verify ADC token acquisition and probe the free countTokens endpoint only when the caller supplies healthCheckModel — never a hardcoded model id. 33 tests across providers/vertex*.test.ts (auth path selection + error mapping via a fake auth client; 401/429/500/unreachable classification; wire-format), all network-free. Gate: pnpm lint && pnpm typecheck && pnpm test = 383 passed | 1 skipped (pre-existing) workspace-wide.

2026-07-12 W2-05 done — role->model matrix + task routing + maker!=verifier defaults (FR-G2, FR-S3): packages/gateway/src/routing/ — matrix.ts resolves a (role, taskType) call to [primary model, ...fallbackChain] from a three-scope (run>project>global) matrix, atomic per role, falling back to the 'default' role when unset; maker-verifier.ts's guardMakerVerifierDistinct refuses a verifier role (code-reviewer/challenger) landing on the same model as the maker (SameModelRefusedError) unless an explicit per-role override setting is true, in which case it mints a MakerVerifierOverrideEvent via an injectable sink; router.ts's route() composes both and makes the guard STRUCTURAL — routing any verifier role auto-resolves the maker role (default coding-agent) for the same task type and compares, so a caller can't silently bypass the refusal by omitting a parameter (fixed after advisor review flagged the initial caller-triggered shape). presets.ts ships All-local/Hybrid/All-cloud, each covering coding-agent/code-reviewer/challenger/test-engineer/pm-interviewer/default; presets.test.ts implements the SRS FR-G2 verify clause verbatim — for every preset and every task type, code-reviewer/challenger never resolve to the coding-agent model. 2026-07-12 W2-06 done — escalation ladder R0-R4 + escalation events (FR-G3): packages/gateway/src/escalation/ — runEscalationLadder (ladder.ts) drives R0 (MemoryConsultHook free consult, ends the ladder with zero events on a hit) -> R1 (matrix chain[0] for role+taskType, default coding-agent/code) -> R2 (chain[1], "one rung up," degenerating to R1's model when the matrix has no fallback) -> R3 (frontier: taskType 'escalation' resolved against a distinct `frontierRole`, default 'challenger' — NOT the climbing role itself, since no shipped preset defines a taskTypes.escalation override on coding-agent, so that would've silently repeated R1's model; caught by advisor review before close, regression-tested via it.each against all three real presets asserting R3 != R1) -> R4 (blocked-with-evidence). The actual model call/micro-loop stays in packages/loop (out of scope, no workspace dep from this write_scope, same constraint W2-05 documented) — an injected AttemptRunner reports back one GateOutcome per rung; this ticket owns rung sequencing, model selection, and event emission only. MissingFailureEvidenceError structurally refuses to advance a rung or reach R4 on a failure reported with zero receipts (evidence-triggered, never vibes-triggered, enforced not just documented). GateOutcome.receiptId threads optionally through to EscalationEvent so a future real AttemptRunner (minting via packages/events' mintReceipt) can be verified against its actual anchored W0-05 receipt rather than trusting the event's copied validator summary. events.ts mirrors routing/maker-verifier.ts's injectable-sink pattern (this package can't depend on packages/events either). isMonotonicRungSequence exposes the "spend rungs are monotonic per ticket" check (TESTING.md §3) for tests. 16 new tests including the SRS-verbatim FR-G3 titles ("a passing ticket can never emit an escalation event", "a simulated R1 gate failure escalates to R2 with the failure receipt attached") plus R4-evidence and degenerate-matrix coverage. 2026-07-12 W2-07 done — budget service: ledger + 70/85/100% breakers (FR-G4, FR-H5): packages/gateway/src/budget/ — CostLedger keyed by project/run/ticket, every entry carrying a berthId so run/project totals aggregate across every berth by construction, never by caller discipline (FR-H5). BudgetBreakerTracker reads the ledger per (project, run), takes the tighter of the run-limit/project-limit ratio (FR-G4: "per run and per project"), and ledgers each of 70% (warn, Record tier), 85% (downshift, Record tier), 100% (hard_stop, Decide tier + approval card carrying US-243's 'budget' risk class) exactly once as spend crosses it — spend is monotonic (a ledger only ever adds cost) so a level once reached is never re-emitted; one large entry that jumps past several thresholds at once still ledgers each one it passed through, in order, at the same final spend, so an audit sees exactly when each tripped (UC-05). policyForLevel() exposes skipOptionalPasses/preferCheaperRungs (true at downshift and above) and canClaimNewTicket (false only at hard_stop, so an in-flight ticket still completes or checkpoints rather than corrupting mid-ticket) — this ticket only decides the policy; enforcement is the consumer's job, same injection-point split as escalation/ladder.ts's AttemptRunner (W3-01's harbormaster ticket loop, which depends_on this ticket, is the intended canClaimNewTicket consumer). events.ts mirrors escalation/events.ts's injectable-sink pattern (noop default + in-memory sink for tests) since this package cannot depend on packages/events from this ticket's write_scope. Not exported from packages/gateway/src/index.ts (out of write_scope, same gap every prior gateway ticket left for its own module). 33 new tests, plain vitest (no fast-check — gateway's existing escalation tests skip it too despite similar monotonicity properties, stayed consistent). Gate: pnpm lint && pnpm typecheck && pnpm test = 464 passed | 1 skipped (pre-existing) workspace-wide.

2026-07-12 W2-07 gate-fix — prior attempt's trackerKey() in packages/gateway/src/budget/breakers.ts embedded a literal raw NUL byte (0x00) as a Map-key separator inside a template literal; git's binary-file heuristic classified the whole file as binary, so `git diff`/`git show` rendered "Binary files differ" for it — defeating blame/log -p and undermining this same file's own UC-05 auditability claim. Fixed by replacing the NUL with a plain `::` delimiter (trackerKey is purely an internal, in-process Map key — never serialized or compared across a boundary, so no behavior change) and re-saving as clean UTF-8 with no control bytes (confirmed: no 0x00 byte in the file, `file` reports "Unicode text, UTF-8 text", and `git diff --text` now renders a normal two-line diff instead of a binary notice). No tests added or changed. Gate re-run green: pnpm lint && pnpm typecheck && pnpm test = 464 passed | 1 skipped (pre-existing) workspace-wide, same counts as the original close.

2026-07-12 W2-08 done — model fitness check: planted-defect bench + fitness cards (FR-G6, BLUEPRINT §12.1): packages/gateway/src/fitness/ — a bench of fixed oracle tasks per role (coding-agent/code-reviewer/challenger, the three named in acceptance) that mints a FitnessCard (fit/unfit/marginal) per (model, role, harnessVersion), keyed per docs/DATABASE.md §7's model_fitness PK. Two oracle kinds: 'function-behavior' is the real executable oracle TESTING.md §8 calls "a bug with a failing test" for the coding-agent task — the model's returned function is extracted via balanced-brace matching and actually invoked (node:vm) against known input/output cases; 'keyword' covers review/challenge, text-reasoning tasks with no executable check for "did it catch the planted issue" (does it cite the planted FIT-DEFECT-1 marker / ask for a citation on the uncited claim). Verified empirically before building on it that vm.Script's `timeout` only bounds the script's own synchronous evaluation, not a later call to a function it returns — calling the returned function host-side hung on an infinite-loop candidate; fixed by wrapping the invocation inside the same timed script text so the call itself is bounded, covered by a test that a while(true) function fails fast instead of hanging the suite. fixtures.ts loads e2e/fitness-fixtures/*.json (plain data, not code) at runtime via fs rather than a cross-package TS import graph; this is this ticket's other write_scope root, not test/fitness/ as TESTING.md §8 names it — plan.json's write_scope governs, same kind of doc-vs-plan.json deviation W2-01 documented. assignment.ts/events.ts satisfy the second acceptance criterion: guardFitAssignment structurally mirrors routing/maker-verifier.ts's guardMakerVerifierDistinct — an unfit or marginal pair throws UnfitAssignmentRefusedError (carrying the card) unless the caller passes an explicit ack, which mints a fitness.unfit_ack event via an injectable sink (this package cannot depend on packages/events from this ticket's write_scope, same constraint every prior gateway ticket hit). Not wired into routing/router.ts's route() (packages/gateway/src/routing/** is outside this ticket's write_scope) and FitnessCardStore is in-memory only (no global SQLite DB owner yet) — both left as HANDOFF in fitness/index.ts for future tickets, same injection-point split W2-05/06/07 left for their own consumers. 46 new tests, including the red-fixture proof this gate needs (TESTING.md §8: "CI tests the harness, not real models") — a scripted strong fake mints 'fit' and a scripted weak fake mints 'unfit' per role against the real e2e/fitness-fixtures/ set, not a synthetic task list. ## 2026-07-12 — W1-07 fixed (symlink-escape) + W3 released

W1-07 blocked on a REAL HIGH: the E2E harbormaster-lite (models the real Harbormaster write-scope enforcement) resolved manifest paths lexically only, so a session could symlink a file inside its scope to a target outside (fs.access follows it), escaping the trust-boundary check the E2E exists to prove. Fixed with fs.realpath re-containment + a failing-capable regression test. The real Harbormaster (W3-01) must use the same symlink-safe resolution. W3 released.

## 2026-07-12 — lessons formalized: finding-loop policy + board deltas

docs/design/FINDING_LOOP_POLICY.md answers "how many loops is too much": findings get identity; iterations classify CLEARED/STALLED/PROGRESSED/OSCILLATING; stall=2 same-tier attempts then escalate (3rd identical attempt always wrong); progress loops budgeted by convergence (ceiling 3+points cap 8; ceiling-while-progressing = decomposition signal); oscillation zero-tolerance; infra failures never charge the coder. Blueprint FR-L6/L7 + SRS rows; tickets W3-08 (ledger+budgets) W3-09 (Harbormaster symlink-safety); W5-07 amended with seam-ownership acceptance. Board 65 tickets / 268 pts.

2026-07-14 W0-09 fix — conductor gate caught a CRITICAL shell-injection PoC in the conformance job: `execSync` interpolated git-tracked `*conformance.test.ts` paths into a shell string via `JSON.stringify`, which escapes quotes/control chars but not shell metacharacters — a path or directory containing `$(...)` got command substitution in the CI runner on every push/PR, including fork `pull_request`s, defeating the exact SC-16 boundary this ticket exists to protect. Fixed by replacing `execSync(\`pnpm exec vitest run ${rels...}\`, ...)` with `execFileSync('pnpm', ['exec', 'vitest', 'run', ...rels], {cwd, stdio: 'inherit'})`, an argv array never parsed by a shell. Verified locally with a live PoC directory named `$(touch pwned)`: no longer executes. Full gate green: pnpm lint && pnpm typecheck && pnpm test = 610 passed | 1 skipped, unchanged (no source touched).

2026-07-15 W3-08 done — Finding ledger + loop-convergence budgets (FR-L6/FR-L7/FR-RL1-4, R-B2/R-D2, US-408/409/410): packages/loop/src/findings*.ts is the finding ledger — stable fingerprint (hash of file/category/normalized-issue), state OPEN/FIX_ATTEMPTED/RESOLVED/REGRESSED/SUPPRESSED, per-finding attempts (bumped only by a STILL_PRESENT recheck, per the FR-L6 fixture sketch), evidence history; `recheck()` rejects INCOMPLETE with zero ledger writes when the verdict lacks a `re-ran independently: <command, counts, exit code>` line (R-B2). Rule-state store (proposed/shadow/advisory/gate/deprecated, human-only transitions — SC-05/FR-RL2), FP-window bookkeeping with auto-demotion flagging (>50% trailing FP), sample-gated promotion; suppression records (justification enum + human signature, FR-RL3) that auto-reopen on context_key change; funnel exposes raw/deduped/effective/suppressed (FR-RL4). An infra-failure taxonomy tracker (unparseable_review/limit_pause/watchdog_kill/output_buffer_overflow, R-D2) is deliberately independent of the ledger — recording one never opens a finding or touches FP metrics. packages/loop/src/loop-policy*.ts is the convergence policy: iteration classifier (CLEARED/STALLED/PROGRESSED/MIXED/OSCILLATING) off the design doc's ledger-signature table; per-finding stall budget (2 same-tier attempts then escalate, +1 post-escalation attempt then BLOCK, REGRESSED escalates immediately, a second REGRESSED blocks — FR-L7 §4 rows 1/2/5); tier-aware progress ceiling (`3 + points`, capped 8 on metered/frontier, floored 12+ on local — FINDING_LOOP_POLICY §3) with park-as-decomposition-signal on ceiling-while-PROGRESSED; sliding 2-pass convergence window (flat/rising open count with no PROGRESSED pass = STOP_DIVERGED); the subjective-score asymmetry (>=7 accept, 5-6 bounded polish, 1-4 escalate-to-human — `classifyReviewSignal`'s return type has no FAIL variant, so it cannot auto-fail a passing deterministic gate, mirroring calibration.ts's `gateDecision` proof pattern). Both modules are pure engine (like coverage.ts/calibration.ts): records and event-shaped payloads only, no event-log writes, no model calls. Fixtures in packages/loop/test/findings.fixture.test.ts wire the real FindingLedger + LoopConvergenceTracker together (not stubs) to prove the two named acceptance scenarios: a 2->9->15 discovering loop runs CONTINUE/PROGRESSED across three passes while every prior resolves, and a flat open-count for two passes returns STOP_DIVERGED. 2026-07-15 W3-08 fix — conductor gate caught a HIGH bug in loop-policy-tracker.ts recordPass(): it evaluated regressedFindings and STILL_PRESENT recheckedFindings against the budget tracker in a loop that returned as soon as the FIRST one produced a BLOCK/ESCALATE decision, so every other regressed/still-present finding later in the same pass's arrays never reached budgetTracker.evaluate() at all — its own oscillation/tier-attempt counter silently never advanced and its own BLOCK/ESCALATE was dropped for that pass. Fixed by evaluating every regressedFindings and every STILL_PRESENT recheckedFindings entry against the tracker unconditionally first (two decision arrays), only afterward selecting the most severe outcome to report (second_oscillation BLOCK > post_escalation_stall BLOCK > regression ESCALATE > stall ESCALATE), with `findings` on the returned action now the full set of findings that hit that severity tier, not just the first one encountered. 2 new tests in loop-policy.test.ts reproduce the exact bug shape (multiple regressed findings in one pass; an earlier-escalating finding followed by a later still-present finding in the same array) and assert via the tracker's `peek()` that the later finding's counter genuinely advanced. Gate: pnpm lint && pnpm typecheck && pnpm test = 688 passed | 1 skipped workspace-wide (was 686 passed | 1 skipped).

2026-07-15 W3-16 done — Escalation policy modes engine: ladder/locked/token-gated (D-018): `packages/gateway/src/escalation/policy-types.ts` + `policy.ts` + `policy.test.ts` (write_scope: `policy*` only, alongside the already-done ladder.ts from W2-06). `resolveEscalationPolicy` resolves a three-scope (run>project>global), per-role policy, defaulting to `ladder` per D-018. `runEscalationPolicy` dispatches: `ladder` delegates outright to ladder.ts's unchanged `runEscalationLadder` (byte-identical behavior, no branch added inside it); `locked` retries the SAME pinned tier under FR-L7's tier-aware convergence ceiling (8 attempts on metered/frontier tiers, 12 on local/owned-hardware) and never advances a rung, so it can never emit an escalation event — a failure past the ceiling parks blocked-with-evidence (`parkedReason: 'locked_ceiling_reached'`) instead of climbing; `token-gated` climbs exactly like `ladder` up to a named tier, then requires a minted `'escalation'`-risk-class token (FR-N2) before crossing it — absent a token it parks (`'awaiting_escalation_token'`, never auto-escalates), a granted token climbs exactly one rung and then resumes normal evidence-triggered climbing beyond it; an optional `resume` input lets a caller continue a parked run from its prior attempts instead of re-running already-failed rungs. Both new modes reuse ladder.ts's exported `MissingFailureEvidenceError` so a receipt-less failure is refused identically in every mode (FR-G3 unaffected by policy, acceptance criterion 2). 26 tests in policy.test.ts, including a dedicated property-style section: deterministic-gate checks across all three modes, a check that routing's independent maker!=verifier guard (`routing/maker-verifier.ts`) behaves identically whether or not a policy mode just ran (nothing in this module touches that guard), and a NEVER-AUTO sweep proving token-gated can never cross a named-tier boundary without an actually-minted token across every (namedTier, gate-outcome) combination. HANDOFF (out of write_scope, `policy*` glob only): `escalation/index.ts`'s barrel (and therefore `packages/gateway/src/index.ts`, wired by W3-11) does not yet re-export `policy.ts` — the same out-of-scope-barrel gap that module's own header comment already documents; the ticket that wires a real consumer (W3-01c, the harbormaster ticket loop) should add the barrel export when it starts calling `runEscalationPolicy`. Gate: pnpm lint && pnpm typecheck && pnpm test = 714 passed | 1 skipped (87 test files) workspace-wide.

2026-07-15 W3-13 done — Secrets vault + redaction + close-gate scanner (BLUEPRINT §12.5, SC-06, NFR-4, pulled forward from W8-03 by AM-2): `packages/shared/src/secrets/**` — `createProjectSecretsVault` wraps the W0-07 `CredentialStore` (keychain/encrypted-file backends) with named registration (`register`/`get`/`delete`); values live only in the credential store, and a names-only index file under `DOKIMA_HOME` (`secrets-index.json`) makes `listNames()`/`listValues()` possible since neither keychain backend exposes a cheap "list all". `patterns.ts` defines the known live-credential shapes (gh tokens, PEM private-key blocks, AWS access key ids, OpenAI/Anthropic-style `sk-` keys, Slack tokens, DB connection strings with embedded creds) — categorically aligned with (but not importing, since it's outside write_scope) the write-path guard in `packages/shared/src/config/settings-files.ts` (`SECRET_LIKE_PATTERNS`). `redact.ts` exposes `redactPatterns`/`redactValues`/`redactString`/`redactDeep` — the last deep-walks any JSON-shaped value and redacts every string leaf, deliberately generic (no import of context-packet or event-payload types, which this ticket doesn't own) so it composes into both destinations named in the acceptance criterion. `env-secrets.ts` loads `.env` file values (never keys) for redaction; `secret-values.ts`'s `collectSecretValues(vault, projectDir)` combines vault + `.env` values into the one list callers feed to `redactDeep`. `content/validators/secrets-scan.sh` is the close-gate scanner, written to the `_lib.sh` contract (exit 0/1 + JSON gaps on stdout) — it scans a project tree for the same pattern categories and, critically, never prints a raw matched secret in a gap (masked to first 4 chars + a length marker), so a validator run cannot itself become the leak SC-06 exists to prevent. Red-fixture proof (packages/shared/src/secrets/secrets-scan-validator.test.ts) spawns the script directly via `bash <path>` (git doesn't reliably preserve the executable bit on `content/`, mirroring `packages/validators/src/run.ts`'s own `resolveCommand()` convention): a planted GitHub token or PEM private key blocks close (exit 1, gap category `github-token`/`pem-private-key`), a clean tree passes (exit 0), and the scanner's own stdout/stderr never contain the raw secret value. 33 new tests (14 redact, 5 env-secrets, 2 secret-values, 6 vault, 6 secrets-scan-validator). HANDOFF (schema bug, both items out of this ticket's write_scope to fix): (1) the write_scope glob `content/validators/secrets-scan*` cannot simultaneously satisfy `packages/validators/src/pack.ts`'s `VALIDATOR_NAME_RE` (`^(validate|run)-.+\.sh$`, landed W1-02) — no filename starts with both `secrets-scan` and `validate-`/`run-`, so this validator is not auto-discovered by `loadValidatorPack()`'s default (no-select) pack load; a future ticket touching `pack.ts` must either widen the regex or rename the file. (2) "wired into every close gate" has no live code path for *any* validator yet — `packages/harbormaster/src/index.ts` is still the W0-01 placeholder; the actual gate wiring (`loadValidatorPack` + `verifyReceipt`'s `requiredValidators` including this name) is the future Harbormaster close-flow ticket's job, consistent with SECURITY_CONTROLS.md's own SC-06 catalog entry. (3) `packages/shared/src/index.ts`/`package.json` (outside write_scope) still need a `./secrets` re-export/subpath, mirroring W3-11's `./config` pattern, for other packages to import this module. 2026-07-15 W3-13 gate-fix — external conductor verification correctly rejected the prior 'done': the two acceptance criteria naming out-of-write_scope files were unmet on any live code path, not just deferred (redaction was never actually called from any packet/event writer; the validator was never actually reachable from any close gate). Reopened to in_progress (this was an agent self-grading error, not a real close). Fixed within write_scope: `createProjectSecretsVault` was not actually project-scoped despite its name — its credential ref and index file carried no project identifier, so two different projects on one machine would silently collide/overwrite each other's registered secrets in the shared keychain namespace and cross-contaminate each other's `listNames()`/`listValues()` (a real security bug for redaction: project A's `.env`/vault values could leak into project B's context via a merged secret-value list). Fixed by threading `projectDir` through `createProjectSecretsVault(store, projectDir, env?)`: a `sha256(path.resolve(projectDir)).slice(0,16)` id now namespaces both the credential ref (`dokima-project-secret:<projectId>:<name>`) and the index file path (`~/.dokima/secrets/<projectId>/secrets-index.json`). New regression test proves cross-project isolation directly against a shared credential store + DOKIMA_HOME. Confirmed genuinely unfixable within this ticket's write_scope (packages/shared/src/secrets/**, content/validators/secrets-scan*) and split out rather than worked around: (1) redaction wiring into the two universal choke points — `packages/loop/src/handoff.ts` (HANDOFF render) and `packages/events/src/append.ts` (event-log write) — is now its own ticket, W3-17, depends_on W3-13; (2) validator-pack discovery/close-gate wiring is now an added acceptance criterion + widened write_scope (`packages/validators/src/pack.ts`) on W3-01b (harbormaster close-gate execution, still todo), which also gained a depends_on W3-13. W3-13 itself stays in_progress — not done — until both land and SC-06 is enforced end-to-end on a real path, per BLUEPRINT's own verify clause. Gate re-run: pnpm lint && pnpm typecheck && pnpm test = 722 passed | 1 skipped workspace-wide (91 test files; was 721 passed | 1 skipped) + `node scripts/validate-plan.mjs` = OK.

2026-07-15 W3-12 done — Shared glob module: one dialect implementation (G-20): `packages/shared/src/glob` is now the single implementation of the write_scope glob dialect — `matcher.ts` (globToRegExp/matchesGlob/matchesAnyGlob, moved verbatim from `packages/git/src/glob.ts`) and `overlap.ts` (globOverlaps/writeScopesOverlap, the segment DP moved verbatim from `packages/tickets/src/lanes.ts`), re-exported through `packages/shared/src/index.ts`. `packages/git/src/glob.ts` is now a one-line re-export shim (kept so `glob.test.ts`/`scope.ts`'s existing imports don't move); `packages/git/src/scope.ts` imports `matchesAnyGlob` from `@dokima/shared` directly. `packages/loop/src/session-scope.ts` drops its hand-duplicated `globToRegExp` (previously justified in a comment by "loop may not import git" — true, but `loop` always could import `shared`, ARCHITECTURE.md §4) and imports it from `@dokima/shared`. `packages/tickets/src/lanes.ts` drops its hand-duplicated segment DP and re-exports `globOverlaps`/`writeScopesOverlap` from `@dokima/shared`, so `lanes.test.ts`/`lanes.property.test.ts` are untouched. All three packages' `package.json` gained a `@dokima/shared: workspace:*` dependency. The acceptance-criterion-2 property test (`packages/shared/src/glob/overlap.property.test.ts`) checks `overlap(a,b)` agrees with an explicit witness path verified independently through `matchesGlob` — both for patterns derived from a shared concrete path and via bounded brute-force witness search over arbitrary small-alphabet pattern pairs (20k+ runs clean); uses a seeded PRNG rather than `fast-check` since `packages/shared/package.json` isn't in this ticket's write_scope (only git/loop/tickets' are). That property test caught a real pre-existing divergence between the two dialect "views": `globToRegExp`'s trailing `/**` required a literal `/` character in the target path (so `dir/**` never matched bare `dir`), while the overlap DP already treated a trailing `**` as zero-or-more segments including zero — exactly the silent-divergence hazard G-20 flagged. Fixed in `matcher.ts`: trailing `/**` now gets the same optional-group regex treatment a mid-pattern `**/` already had, verified against the existing (unmodified) `glob.test.ts`/`scope.test.ts` assertions plus the new property test. 2026-07-15 W4-02 gate-fix — conductor flagged `computeProjectStats()` (`apps/server/src/api/projects.ts`) for a bare `catch { return EMPTY_STATS }` that swallowed every failure uniformly, including genuine bugs and DB corruption, with zero record that anything failed. Fixed by narrowing to `isUnmigratedSchemaError(err)`: a better-sqlite3 `SqliteError` with code `SQLITE_ERROR` and a "no such table|no such column" message is exactly the shape `openEventLogReader` (read-only, no migrations applied) throws against an older-schema `state.db` — the one documented degrade case. Every other error (verified live via a garbage-bytes file → `SQLITE_NOTADB`) now goes through `console.error(dbPath, err)` before still returning `EMPTY_STATS`, so a real fault is never silently invisible. 2026-07-15 W4-04 gate-fix — conductor flagged two HIGH findings on the prior chat-workspace attempt. (1) `ChatView.tsx`'s chat-history fetch effect had no `.catch()`: a rejected `fetchChatEvents` (401/404/network failure) still flipped `loaded` true via `.finally()`, so the UI silently rendered the same "No agent activity yet" empty state as a legitimately-empty thread list, with zero signal a request actually failed. Fixed with a `.catch()` that records the error and a new `chat-load-error` UI state (`ChatView.tsx`, `chat.css`) distinct from `ChatEmptyState`. (2) `Card.tsx`'s `FindingCardBody` rendered `card.evidenceHref` straight into an `<a href>` from wire data arriving over the untrusted `chat:<project>` channel (agent sessions are untrusted, THREAT_MODEL trust boundary) — a compromised agent session could publish a `javascript:`/`data:` URI that executes on click; `ProvenanceLine` already avoided exactly this by building its receipt link itself instead of trusting a raw href, and `FindingCardBody` hadn't followed that precedent. Fixed with `isSafeEvidenceHref` (exported, allowlists the `/api/v1/` prefix only) — an unsafe href now renders inert "evidence unavailable" text instead of a clickable link. New tests: `apps/web/src/chat/Card.test.ts` (6 cases: safe path, `javascript:`, `data:`, absolute external URL, protocol-relative URL, other relative path) + one new Playwright spec in `chat.spec.ts` (route-intercepts the chat GET to return 401, asserts `chat-load-error` renders and `chat-empty-state` does not). Gate: `pnpm lint && pnpm typecheck && pnpm test` = 886 passed | 1 skipped (115 test files) workspace-wide (was 880 passed | 1 skipped; +6 new) + `pnpm --filter web e2e` = 18/18 Playwright specs chromium (was 17/17; +1) + `node scripts/validate-plan.mjs` = OK. Zero out-of-scope files.

A fixture test (`board-routes.test.ts`) proves `accept` succeeds on a ticket claimed/started/closed via direct `@dokima/tickets` calls under an agent actor id (modeling Harbormaster, never touching the board API) and is refused with `SELF_ACCEPT`, explained inline via the RFC 7807 problem body (`rule`/`detail`), on a ticket claimed/started/closed entirely through the board API instead. A card drag can never supply a Completion Manifest, so a drop onto "In Review" always fires `close` with an empty body and is refused with `MANIFEST_INVALID` — demonstrated directly (UX_SPEC §4 "no drag ever bypasses a gate"), not special-cased. `fireTicketVerb` (`board/api.ts`) gained a `projectId` param sent as `?project=`: API_DESIGN's documented `POST /tickets/{id}/{verb}` path has no project segment, and the CLI never needed one (always resolves one dbPath from `cwd`) — a real gap for "one core serving N projects" (D-013) this ticket had to close to be genuinely usable, not just documentation-shaped. `App.tsx`: `BoardView` portals into `pane-board` (same technique W4-04 used for chat). Found and fixed a real pre-existing bug via e2e, not specific to this ticket's own new code: `useChatPaneNode`/`useBoardPaneNode` queried their pane's DOM node once on mount with an empty dependency array — that only works via a full-page deep-link navigation (every `chat.spec.ts` test happens to use exactly that pattern, `page.goto('/?project=...')`), so it never surfaced there; the primary Fleet → click "Open" flow never reloads the page, so the query ran while still on Fleet, before `SplitPaneWorkspace`'s panes existed, and cached a permanent `null` — silently breaking both chat's and board's content mounting on the actual primary user path. Fixed by re-running the query whenever `projectId` changes. `board.spec.ts` (4 new e2e specs against the real server + a real per-project `state.db`): live-projection rendering (lanes × columns × typed cards, green receipt dot), the UX_SPEC §2b empty state, a real drag firing `claim`, and a real drag refused inline with `MANIFEST_INVALID`; fixtures seeded via `fixtures/seed-board-tickets.mjs`, which dynamic-imports `packages/*/src/index.ts` by absolute `file://` URL under `tsx` (`apps/web` can't depend on `@dokima/tickets`/`@dokima/events` directly — `package.json` is outside this ticket's write_scope — and no ticket-creation REST endpoint or CLI subcommand exists to seed through instead). Needed a wide viewport (`test.use`) for real drags onto columns past the board pane's roughly-one-third-of-window visible width; a chained second `dragTo()` within one test proved unreliable in Playwright's Chromium HTML5 drag simulation regardless of what happened between the two drags, so each test performs at most one real drag (seeding or the card-menu keyboard-equivalent gets a card to its starting state otherwise). (1) `ticketCountForProject` (`estimate-routes.ts`) counted every ticket the project's projection has ever seen, including `done`/`waived` ones, so `GET /estimate` and `POST /estimate/what-if` conflated money already spent with money about to be spent. Fixed: now sums only tickets whose status is not `done`/`waived` before pricing; new test drives a ticket through the full `claim`→`start`→`close`→`accept` lifecycle to `done` and asserts it's excluded from both the count and the wave-0 bucket (11→12 tests in that describe block). (2) `packages/gateway/src/estimate/**` (the pure `computeWaveEstimate` engine with historical-actuals override + real per-wave grouping) was unreachable from any live route — its own module header's claim that "`packages/loop` already depends on gateway" was false (grep-verified zero references outside its own test files) — while the live `GET /projects/:id/estimate` route hand-duplicated a strictly weaker version (matrix-only, single fabricated wave). Investigated wiring the live route to the real engine instead (`import computeWaveEstimate from '@dokima/gateway'`): empirically confirmed this is blocked by write_scope — the import throws `Cannot find package '@dokima/gateway'` without a dependency declaration in `apps/server/package.json`, and making that dependency resolvable needs edits to *both* `apps/server/package.json` and `packages/gateway/package.json`'s `exports` map (it only exports `.` → `src/index.ts`, and `estimate/index.ts` is explicitly not re-exported there); neither file is in this ticket's `write_scope`, and `conductor.config.json`'s `alwaysOk` list only covers the literal root `package.json` (`^package\.json$`), not any nested one — confirmed by reading its glob compilation, not assumed. Also confirmed wiring it up wouldn't have changed live behavior anyway: `board-wire.ts`'s `wave` is always 0 (no wave producer on the `Ticket` model) and there is no historical-actuals source reachable from `apps/server` (no spend-ledger migration), so the sophisticated engine would collapse to the exact same single-bucket, matrix-only output the hand-duplicated version already produces — the gap is a data-model gap, not a smarter-estimate-function gap. Deleted `packages/gateway/src/estimate/**` (10 files: 4 source + 4 co-located tests + `types.ts` + `index.ts` barrel) rather than leave it as unreachable, falsely-documented dead code, and rewrote `estimate-routes.ts`'s module header to state plainly, with citations, what AC-1 delivers live today (matrix-price, single wave-0 bucket of tickets still pending autorun, no historical override) and exactly why the rest (real multi-wave buckets, historical actuals) isn't yet possible. D-014/L-28 shadow-calibration: `classifySecretsGaps` only gates on a gap whose file is in the diff's `changedPaths` — a pre-existing fixture/doc self-hit in a file this session never touched is reported (raw count never hidden) but doesn't block. SECURITY (CRITICAL, review-caught fail-open on the prior attempt, now fixed): `effectiveValidatorResults` previously called `classifySecretsGaps` unconditionally; `runValidator`'s 0/1/2 contract returns exit=2 for timeout/spawn-error/malformed-output/contract-violation with a synthetic gap detail that isn't file:line shaped, so the diff-scope filter would parse it as "no location match," find zero hits, and reclassify a scanner that never produced a trustworthy verdict as a false-clean exit 0 — a hung/crashed scanner would sail straight through to a minted receipt. Fixed with an explicit short-circuit (`if (result.exitCode === 2) return result` BEFORE `classifySecretsGaps` runs) so exit=2 is always preserved as a hard failure. Red fixture: a secrets-scan fixture that sleeps past `validatorTimeoutMs` (forced timeout → exit 2) blocks close with no receipt minted, ticket left `in_progress` — same "no close receipt ⇒ failure comment" contract as every other refusal path. 13 tests in `loop-gates.test.ts` (happy-path receipt mint + `verifyReceipt` roundtrip; promise-token evidence strings granting no bypass; claimed-but-uncommitted spoofed manifest; claimed-file-never-existed stat check; verify re-run ignoring the manifest's claimed exit; R-G2 memory_written[] inert-until-W7-01 check; a NEW planted secret in the diff blocking close via the real gate path; a pre-existing secret in an untouched file NOT blocking; the exit=2 fail-closed fixture) + `parseGapLocation`/`classifySecretsGaps` unit tests. HANDOFF (out of write_scope, for whichever ticket next touches `content/validators/secrets-scan.sh`, owned by the W3-13 lineage): the scanner's per-file re-grep (`grep -oE ... | head -1`) has a real SIGPIPE bug under `_lib.sh`'s inherited `set -euo pipefail` — verified directly (`bash -c 'set -euo pipefail; matched="$(grep ... | head -1)"'` against a fixture with ~20k matches in one file exits 141, not the intended 0/1/2 contract). Acceptance 5's "guard the per-file re-grep against SIGPIPE" goal is met from this ticket's side of the wall, not by patching the shell script (out of scope): a SIGPIPE crash produces no valid JSON, so `runValidator` already normalizes it to exit=2 (malformed-output), and this ticket's new short-circuit guarantees that's never reclassified as clean — the 0/1/2 contract holds end-to-end even though the shell-side bug itself is still latent. The colon-in-filename half of acceptance 5 IS fixed in-scope: `parseGapLocation` matches the line number greedily from the end (`/^(.*):(\d+)$/`), covered by a dedicated test. 2026-07-18 W3-09 done — Harbormaster symlink-safe scope resolution + TOCTOU-closed read (W1-07 class, BLUEPRINT §3.6/SC-01): `packages/harbormaster/src/scope.ts` (`classifyManifestFile`) resolves every manifest-claimed path via `fs.realpath` and refuses containment escapes (leaf or ancestor-directory symlink) before anything is stat-ed, read, or hashed — then, having verified containment, opens the resolved target ONCE with `O_NOFOLLOW` and does fstat + read from that SAME held `FileHandle`, never re-resolving the manifest path string a second time (closes the review-caught HIGH TOCTOU: a background process racing `ln -sf /etc/passwd file` between the old separate realpath-check and readFile calls could smuggle an out-of-root file into a receipt). Wired into the real close gate (`loop-gates.ts`) at both the initial classification (replacing `statMissingFiles`) and the read-time re-check before `mintReceipt`. Red fixtures: a committed symlink escaping the worktree fails close-gate verification (`loop-gates.test.ts`); a file swapped for an escaping symlink by the ticket's own verify command between the initial check and the read is still refused (`loop-gates.test.ts` TOCTOU fixture); a deterministic single-fd race fixture and a 100-call adversarial-swap-loop fixture both prove the narrower fd-level TOCTOU is closed, verified failing against the prior two-syscall shape (`scope.test.ts`). Gate: `pnpm lint && pnpm typecheck && pnpm test` = 1422 passed | 1 skipped workspace-wide, 190 test files (20 in packages/harbormaster, +25 net: scope.test.ts new at 10, loop-gates.test.ts +2 to 15).
2026-07-18 W3-09 gate-fix — review caught that the prior attempt's fix only closed the LEAF variant of the TOCTOU: `classifyManifestFile` still computed containment via a path-string `fs.realpath(absPath)` and then re-opened that same string a second time (`fs.open(real, O_NOFOLLOW)`) — `O_NOFOLLOW` only guards the trailing path component, so an ANCESTOR directory swapped to a symlink between the two calls was never caught (the held fd's only post-open check was `stat.isFile()`, never containment). Fixed by adding `fdStillWithinRoot()`, run on the already-open fd immediately before the read: (1) an `lstat` walk of every ancestor of the checked `real` path, refusing if any is currently a symlink, and (2) an `fstat(fd)`-vs-`lstat(path)` `{dev,ino}` identity comparison, refusing on mismatch — this second check is what catches an ancestor swapped to an escaping symlink for the open and then swapped BACK before the walk runs, which the walk alone would miss. Verified empirically (throwaway script, this box) that no portable fd-to-path/openat primitive exists in pure Node: macOS's `/dev/fd/<n>` does not dereference to a real path and does not support the Linux-only `/proc/self/fd/<n>/subpath` relative-open trick, and `node:fs` (v22) exposes no `openat` — so this is the closest cross-platform approximation, documented as such in `scope.ts`'s module comment. Two new red fixtures in `scope.test.ts` (10 → 14 tests): a deterministic ancestor-swapped-between-check-and-open fixture (reuses the existing `afterRealpathBeforeOpen` seam, per the gate finding's explicit ask) and a swap-then-restore-before-recheck fixture targeting the identity-mismatch path specifically (new `afterOpenBeforeContainmentRecheck` seam). `loop-gates.ts` needed no changes — it already routes every manifest path exclusively through `classifyManifestFile`/`classifyManifestFiles`, so the fix is centralized in `scope.ts`. Full gate: `pnpm lint && pnpm typecheck && pnpm test` = 1424 passed | 1 skipped workspace-wide, 190 test files (harbormaster unchanged at 20; scope.test.ts 10 → 14). `node scripts/validate-plan.mjs` = OK. `node scripts/validate-traceability.mjs` = OK (same 7 pre-existing coverage gaps, none touching this ticket). `validate-file-size.sh`'s only 2 gaps (`scripts/conductor.mjs`, `packages/events/src/receipts.ts`) are pre-existing, outside this ticket's diff — `scope.ts` is 232 lines. `validate-circular-deps.sh` = 0 gaps. Out-of-scope check: diff is exactly `scope.ts`/`scope.test.ts` plus `plan.json`/`docs/STATUS.md` board bookkeeping.

Called back-to-back with `claimTicket` with no `await` between them, this is race-free across concurrently-running berths in one process: Node is single-threaded and `EventLog` reads/writes are synchronous (better-sqlite3), so no other berth's code can run inside that window — exactly BLUEPRINT §5's "the claim step re-checks lane occupancy atomically... no distributed locking needed." `berths-identity.ts`: `ensureBerthIdentities` mints one machine identity per berth (DATABASE.md §2), idempotently keyed by `(runId, berthIndex)`. `berths.ts`: `runBerths` runs N concurrent berths, each with its own machine identity and (ticket-id-keyed, therefore already isolated) git worktree; `stopSwitch`/`breakerLevel` are shared callbacks across every berth (mirrors `loop-claim.ts`/`loop-land.ts`'s own injection shape) so a kill-file or a tripped budget breaker halts every berth at its own next ticket boundary — `@dokima/gateway`'s `CostLedger` already aggregates spend by `(project, run)` without filtering by berth, so no new aggregation logic was needed, just passing one shared instance to every berth; `shouldPauseAtBreakpoint` (`breakpoints.ts`, previously unwired) is now composed in, so a landed ticket can pause the whole run once berths=N crosses a `ticket`/`wave` breakpoint boundary. 2026-07-18 W3-04 gate-fix — review caught that `runBerths` aggregated all berths with `Promise.all(identities.map(...runOneBerth...))`: if any awaited call inside one berth's loop threw (most plausibly the injected `runTicket`, but also `resolveBerthWorktree`/`createWorktree` or a throwing `stopSwitch`/`breakerLevel`), `Promise.all` rejected immediately and `runBerths` returned to the caller while the other N-1 berths' `runOneBerth` promises kept running unobserved in the background — still claiming/starting/closing tickets and creating worktrees with no handle left to halt them, undercutting the ticket's own halt semantics. Fixed by wrapping each berth's whole loop body in a `try`/`catch`: on catch, the berth records the error on a new `SharedBerthState.error` field (alongside the existing `paused` flag) and returns its own outcome with a new `BerthStopReason: 'error'` instead of rejecting; every other berth checks `shared.error` at its own next loop-top boundary (same place it already checks `stopSwitch`/`paused`/the budget breaker) and halts the same way, finishing whatever ticket it already held but never claiming another — so `runOneBerth` never rejects and `Promise.all` always resolves with every berth's real outcome, including which error halted the run (`BerthOutcome.error`). New test: one of 4 berths' `runTicket` throws immediately while the other 3 are mid-ticket on an 8-lane board — asserts all 4 stop with `stopReason: 'error'`/the same `Error` reference, the 3 in-flight tickets land (`in_review`) before their berths stop, the thrown ticket is left `in_progress` (never auto-blocked, since the throw happens before that code path), and the remaining 4 of 8 seeded tickets stay untouched (`ready`) — proving no berth claims a second ticket once another has errored. Also fixed the still-present R-02 (try/catch inside loop) in `test/berths-test-helpers.ts`'s `forEachSeed`/`forEachSeedAsync`: hoisted the `try` outside the `for` loop (the failing-seed number is now captured via an outer-scoped `let seed` read in the `catch`), a cosmetic refactor with no behavior change (the prior code was already flagged as a non-blocking false positive — small sync/async loop bodies, not a hot path). Full gate: `pnpm lint && pnpm typecheck && pnpm test` = 1497 passed | 1 skipped (201 test files) workspace-wide (+1 net: the new throwing-berth test). `node scripts/validate-plan.mjs` = OK. `node scripts/validate-traceability.mjs` = OK (same 7 pre-existing coverage gaps, none touching this ticket). Out-of-scope check: diff is exactly `berths.ts`, `berths-test-helpers.ts`, `berths.test.ts` plus `plan.json`/`docs/STATUS.md` board bookkeeping.

2026-07-18 W4-05 gate-fix — conductor re-verification of the already-landed W4-05 found a CRITICAL git-option-injection gap the prior pass missed: `showAtRev()`/`isSymlinkAtRev()` (`git-read.ts`) built git argv as `['show', \`${rev}:${filePath}\`]` / `['ls-tree', rev, ...]` with the route-supplied `rev`/`from`/`to` (`GET .../artifacts/doc?rev=`, `GET .../artifacts/doc-diff?from=&to=`) passed straight through unchecked — only `path` was validated via `isSafeRelativePath`. A value starting with `-` is parsed by git as an *option* rather than a revision; `git show --output=/tmp/pwned.txt:f.txt` writes the blob content to an attacker-chosen path (git's diff-options `--output=<file>` is honored by `git show`), an arbitrary-file-write primitive reachable by any bearer-token holder including a compromised agent session (law #4) — verified exploitable locally (the file *was* written) before fixing, then reconfirmed refused after. Fixed with `isSafeGitRevision(rev)` (non-empty, not `-`-prefixed) in `artifacts-helpers.ts`, enforced at both the `/artifacts/doc` and `/artifacts/doc-diff` route handlers (400 on failure) and defensively inside `showAtRev()` itself so no future caller can bypass it (this also covers `isSymlinkAtRev`, which is only ever reached through `showAtRev`). `/artifacts/diff`'s `ticket` query param was never vulnerable — it only feeds `findTicketBranch`'s `sw/<ticket>*` glob pattern, which structurally can't start with `-`. Two new red-fixture tests (`rev=--output=<path>`, `from`/`to=--output=<path>`) assert 400 and that no file was written. Gate: `pnpm lint && pnpm typecheck && pnpm test` = 1575 passed | 1 skipped (209 test files) workspace-wide (+2 net: the new red-fixture tests; `artifacts-routes.test.ts` now 17 tests). `validate-file-size.sh`: `artifacts-routes.ts` still 384/400 lines; its reported 2 gaps (`scripts/conductor.mjs`, `packages/events/src/receipts.ts`) are pre-existing and out of write_scope (confirmed via `git log`, both predate this ticket). `validate-plan.mjs`/`validate-traceability.mjs` = OK. Playwright e2e: `playwright.config.ts` sets no `workers` cap and every spec file shares one `webServer`/`state.db`, so default (unbounded) parallel workers flake across every spec — fleet/board/roster/artifacts alike, not just the routes touched here; confirmed this is a pre-existing environment property (not a regression from this fix) by reproducing the identical failure with the fix stashed out, and by driving project-creation manually against the live server (works) and via curl (201/200, no server error). `--workers=1` (serializing spec files against the shared server) passes all 34: 31 passed, 3 skipped — the same count the prior landing reported. `playwright.config.ts` is outside this ticket's write_scope, so the workers cap isn't added here. Out-of-scope check: diff is exactly `artifacts-helpers.ts`, `artifacts-routes.ts`, `git-read.ts`, `artifacts-routes.test.ts`, plus `plan.json`/`docs/STATUS.md` board bookkeeping.

2026-07-18 W6-02 done — Gitea adapter + generic git fallback (FR-I2). `packages/forge/src/gitea*.ts` implements the same `ForgeAdapter` contract as W6-01's GitHub adapter (repo ops, branch protection + SC-14 drift validator, PR lifecycle, issue mirror, commit status, maker/reviewer identity), same book-style chapter split, wire shapes verified 2026-07-18 against the live Gitea OpenAPI spec (`gitea.com/swagger.v1.json`) and `docs.gitea.com`'s API-usage guide rather than assumed GitHub-shaped. Load-bearing deltas documented in `gitea-types.ts`'s header and enforced by fixtures: `Authorization: token <t>` (not `Bearer`); no `allow_deletions` toggle — protected branches are structurally undeletable, so the drift snapshot always reports `false` and a caller-requested `allowDeletions: true` surfaces as honest drift rather than being silently accepted; `enforceAdmins` maps to `block_admin_merge_override` (best-fit field-name inference — Gitea's swagger carries no prose description for it); PR merge (`POST .../merge`) returns an empty 200 body, so `mergePullRequest` re-fetches the PR afterward for `merge_commit_sha`/`merged`; issue create/edit bodies have no name-keyed `labels` field, so labels route through the dedicated `PUT .../issues/{n}/labels` endpoint (which accepts name strings) as a follow-up call, and the adapter reports back the names Gitea actually applied; Gitea's `Issue` has no `state_reason`, so `stateReason` is always `null`; no native rate-limit signal (no `x-ratelimit-*`), so only a proxy-emitted `Retry-After` is honored. `packages/forge/src/generic-git.ts` is the honest-degradation fallback for SSH-only repos with no forge behind them (docs/design/CONTRACTS.md §2): `capabilities()` truthfully reports all-false, and every operation — including `getRepo()`, which has no bare-SSH equivalent for `private`/`archived`/`permissions` — rejects with a typed `GenericGitUnsupportedError` instead of no-op'ing or fabricating a value (D-014, never silent). The actual local-merge degrade path is deliberately left to the caller (`packages/git`'s `mergeLocal()`, via a future landing/harbormaster ticket): `packages/forge/package.json` declares zero dependencies and is outside this ticket's `gitea*`/`generic*` write_scope to change, so shelling out to git from inside this adapter would require an undocumented dependency addition — noted here rather than worked around. Behavioral parity with `github-*.test.ts`'s coverage (configure/drift/PR/issues/status/error-mapping, incl. the same RED drift fixtures) via mirrored per-chapter tests, not a literal single shared contract-suite runner across both adapters — building or wiring one would touch `github*.ts`/`types.ts`, outside this ticket's write_scope; documented here rather than silently claimed as literal FR-I2 text. 33 new tests (`packages/forge/src/gitea*.test.ts` + `generic-git.test.ts`). Gate: `pnpm lint && pnpm typecheck && pnpm test` = 1746 passed | 1 skipped (231 test files) workspace-wide (was 1693/1/224 at W4-13; +53: 33 new forge tests + net growth from other tickets landed on main since). `node scripts/validate-plan.mjs`/`validate-traceability.mjs` not re-run this session (no board-schema or FR-mapping change). Out-of-scope check: diff is exactly the 18 new `packages/forge/src/gitea*.ts`/`generic-git*.ts` files, plus `plan.json`/`docs/STATUS.md` board bookkeeping — zero unexplained files.

Added `packages/forge/src/gitea-parity.ts` (`checkForgeAdapterParity`: structural key-path diff between two adapters' outputs for the same operation — asserts shape parity, not value equality, since adapter-specific values legitimately differ) plus `packages/forge/src/gitea-parity.test.ts` proving it: a clean pass between the real adapters, and two RED cases (a dropped field, a renamed field) planted via minimal `ParityCheckedAdapter` doubles — the literal "induced divergence" the Proof line requires. (Caught during authoring: the first draft of the RED-case doubles used object-spread on a class instance to override one method — spread only copies OWN enumerable properties and class methods live on the prototype, so it type-checked but threw at runtime; fixed by narrowing `checkForgeAdapterParity`'s parameter to `Pick<ForgeAdapter,'capabilities'|'getRepo'>` and building explicit minimal doubles instead of spreading.) 2026-07-18 W4-11 gate-fix — external review of the DONE 2026-07-18 close found two HIGH findings, both fixed. (1) `board-routes.ts`: the verb-route replay key (`${verb}:${ticketId}:${idempotencyKey}`) omitted the project id even though `IdempotencyStore` is one instance shared across every project `buildApiServer` serves — two projects reusing the same ticket id + Idempotency-Key (e.g. a CLI script's `T-1`/`k-1` pattern) collided, and the second request silently replayed the first project's stored response instead of applying the verb. Fixed to `${projectId}:${verb}:${ticketId}:${idempotencyKey}`; new regression test registers two projects each with a `T-1` ticket, fires the same `claim` + Idempotency-Key against both, and asserts project B's log shows a real `ticket.claimed` event (not a cross-project replay). (2) `artifacts-routes.ts`: `POST /api/v1/projects/:id/artifacts/comments` (in write_scope, a mutating POST) had no Idempotency-Key/X-Event-Seq handling at all, so acceptance criterion 2 ("accepted on every mutating POST") wasn't actually true for this route — undisclosed in the prior close's notes, unlike the verb-route gap. Wired the same `IdempotencyStore`/`extractIdempotencyKey` pattern (key scoped by project id + client-supplied Idempotency-Key, since this isn't a lifecycle-verb route), echoing `X-Event-Seq` on every comment POST (the comment event's seq, or the `revision.requested` event's seq when the deliverable is gated) and replaying the stored response verbatim on a repeated key. Hoisted `EVENT_SEQ_HEADER` into `idempotency.ts` (now shared by `board-routes.ts` and `artifacts-routes.ts`) instead of duplicating the header-name constant a second time. Token-delivery residual and the out-of-write_scope `projects.ts` fleet.json gap are unchanged from the 2026-07-18 DONE note — `projects.ts` still matches none of this ticket's write_scope globs, so it stays a forward HANDOFF rather than scope-creep. (2) `validate-code-health.sh` R-02 (try/catch inside loop) kept flagging `flushMirrorQueue`'s per-entry try/catch even though it's load-bearing (needed to know which entry failed so `remaining` retries just that one, and to preserve already-flushed results) — the heuristic is a pure textual "try{ within 5 lines of a for/while/map/etc" grep with no suppression mechanism. Fixed by extracting the try/catch into a standalone `flushEntry` helper (returns a success/error union instead of throwing) defined above the loop; the loop itself now has no try/catch in its body, same drain/retry/error-propagation behavior, confirmed identical via the existing `queue.test.ts` suite unchanged. 2026-07-19 W6-04 done — MCP host (FR-I3, SC-12, US-503): `packages/mcp` built as a pure events projection (ARCHITECTURE.md §4 law 4 — no SQL table of its own, `loadMcpState` folds `mcp.server_registered`/`mcp.allowlist_set`/`approval.requested`/`approval.decided`/`mcp.tool_call.completed`/`mcp.tool_call.denied` from the log). `registerServer`/`setRoleAllowlist` are the config verbs (id/tool-id collision refused). `requestToolCall` is the single entry an agent's tool-call request flows through: refuses tools missing from the role's allowlist (AC-1, verified both as invisible via `listToolsForRole` and refused via `TOOL_NOT_ALLOWED`); when the tool's `requiresApproval` (static or `'dynamic'`, resolved by an injectable `DynamicApprovalPolicy` with a real fail-closed `defaultShellApprovalPolicy` heuristic for shell) is true, it parks an `approval.requested` card and never touches the injected `ToolExecutor` (AC-2, proven via executor-spy zero-call assertions) — `decideToolCall` resolves it, refusing self-approval (`decidedBy === requestedBy`, mirroring `@dokima/tickets`' `SELF_ACCEPT`, T-14). Every terminal call state (immediate completion, or post-approval completion, or denial) appends exactly one audited `mcp.tool_call.completed`/`denied` event carrying an args digest + result digest (sha256 of a canonical key-sorted JSON form, computed before `appendEvent`'s redaction) + cost (AC-3); a fast-check property suite proves rebuild-from-zero equals incremental apply across arbitrary event sequences, including `approval.*` events carrying a foreign `domain` (ignored via discriminator, so this reducer never mis-folds an unrelated future approval feature). The `approval.requested`/`approval.decided` event names are ARCHITECTURE.md §3's own already-declared vocabulary, not invented here — wiring them into an actual visible card (apps/server's `NotificationKind: 'approval'` / harbormaster's `CARD_KINDS: 'approval'` both already exist) is a HANDOFF outside `packages/mcp/**`. Real MCP transport (stdio/HTTP spawn) is deliberately kept out of this package — `ToolExecutor` is caller-injected, matching C-1 (no network in tests) and the absence of any MCP SDK in `docs/TECH_STACK.md` to verify against. Self-review before commit caught two real issues: (1) `decideToolCall`'s approved path appended `approval.decided` *before* awaiting the executor — a throw left the card stuck `'approved'` with no terminal event and no retry path (`APPROVAL_NOT_PENDING` on redrive); fixed by moving that append to after the executor resolves, so a failure leaves the card `'pending'` and retryable (denied path untouched, no async there). (2) the approved path executes with `approval.args`, the log-redacted copy (`appendEvent` -> `redactDeep` already ran on it when `approval.requested` was stored, law 8) — correct trust-boundary behavior (a secret-shaped arg never reaches the executor raw) but previously undocumented and unpinned; fixed with a doc comment on `PendingApproval.args` plus a test proving a planted `sk-...` token is redacted before both the card and the executor see it. 37 new tests across 6 files (`digest`, `approval-policy`, `register`, `tool-call`, `reducer.property`, `index`). Added `@dokima/events` (dependency) + `fast-check` (devDependency) to `packages/mcp/package.json`, matching `packages/tickets`' convention — required a `pnpm-lock.yaml` regen (workspace-only, no registry fetch). 2026-07-19 W6-04 gate-fix — prior close's gate-failure note: `requestToolCall` never checked whether `input.id` collides with an existing pending/decided approval or a previously completed call before appending `approval.requested`/`mcp.tool_call.completed` — `reducer.ts`'s `approval.requested` case unconditionally does `pendingApprovals.set(approval.id, approval)`, so a reused id could silently reset an already-`approved`/`denied` card back to `pending` or duplicate a completed call's audit record; `register.ts` already guarded the analogous case (`SERVER_ALREADY_REGISTERED`/`TOOL_ID_COLLISION`) but the same discipline was missing here. Fixed in `tool-call.ts`: new `requireCallIdAvailable(state, id)` checks `state.pendingApprovals.has(id)` and `state.callLog.some(r => r.id === id)`, throwing a new `McpError` code `CALL_ID_COLLISION` (added to `errors.ts`'s `McpErrorCode` union); called once in `requestToolCall` right after `requireAllowed`, before either the approval-parking branch or the immediate-completion branch. Added 2 tests: a reused id against an already-completed call (executor invoked exactly once, second call throws before touching the executor again) and a reused id against an existing pending approval (executor never invoked, original card's `argsDigest` untouched). 2026-07-19 W6-06 gate-fix — CRITICAL: `withSeatbeltProfile` (`packages/harbormaster/src/sandbox/process.ts`) deleted the temp dir holding the darwin Seatbelt profile in its `finally` block before the sandboxed process was ever spawned — the `run` callback it wrapped only built argv via `resolveInvocation()`, while the actual `spawn()` happened later in `runInProcessSandbox`, after the profile file was already gone. Empirically reproduced pre-fix: `runInProcessSandbox({cwd: '/tmp', command: 'echo hi'})` under the default (network-denied) profile returned exitCode 65, stderr `sandbox-exec: .../<uuid>.sb: No such file or directory` — the default process profile could not run ANY command on macOS, not just network-touching ones, yet the existing "denies network by default" tests passed anyway because their assertions (`exitCode !== 0`, stdout lacks marker) are equally satisfied by sandbox-exec failing to start at all. Fixed by extracting the spawn+wait into `spawnAndWait()` and calling it from inside `withSeatbeltProfile`'s `run` callback, so the profile dir stays alive for the full child lifetime and is only `rm`'d after the child settles. Strengthened `process.test.ts`/`index.test.ts`: both network-denial tests now also assert `stderr` does not match `/sandbox-exec|No such file or directory/`, and a new benign-command sanity test (`echo hi` under the default profile must exit 0) was added — confirmed via a git-stash revert that this new test fails loudly (exit 65) against the pre-fix code and passes post-fix. [CRITICAL] `runNightlyVerify` fed the 10/12 zero-filled catalog fields straight into the real `verifyItem`, whose predicate for those fields is always `== 0`/`<= 3` — an unconditional false-positive `done` for any accepted item resting on a field with no live producer, not a genuine re-verification. [HIGH] Neither loop wrapped its per-project body in try/catch, and `pollRunCompletions` advanced the cursor before calling `evaluatePlan` — one project's transient failure (locked `state.db`, malformed catalog) silently skipped every other registered project that tick and permanently lost that trigger. Fixed: both loops now wrap each project's body in try/catch (`onError(projectPath, err)`, defaulting to `console.error`), and `pollRunCompletions` only advances a project's cursor after its pass succeeds (at-least-once retry next poll — safe since `evaluatePlan`/`proposeFromMatches` is idempotent per catalogId). `startPlanScheduler` threads both new callbacks through unchanged existing signatures (`onError(phase, err)` still works for the fleet-level `listProjectCards` failure path the "stop() clears both timers" test exercises). 9 new tests (`snapshot.test.ts` +6 covering `referencedSnapshotPaths`/`unresolvedSnapshotPaths`/`LIVE_SNAPSHOT_PATHS` against every one of the 12 real catalog verify criteria; `plan-scheduler.test.ts` +3: one project's `evaluatePlan` failure doesn't sink another project's poll and its cursor isn't advanced past the trigger; a caller-supplied non-live-field accepted item is skipped, not fake-flipped to `done`, with the skip reported; one project's `verifyPlan` failure doesn't block another project's nightly verify), using `vi.mock` with `importOriginal` passthrough on `../api/plans-store.js` (first use of module mocking in `apps/server`, scoped to overriding only `evaluatePlan`/`verifyPlan` for one-shot rejections — everything else stays the real filesystem-backed implementation). 2026-07-19 W5-03 gate-fix — conductor's HIGH finding fixed: `formatLedgerRow` (packages/pipeline/src/decisions/ledger.ts) only ran `escapeCell` on the decision/optionsConsidered/rationale cells, leaving `record.id`/`record.date` interpolated raw. `DecisionRecord.id`/`.date` are plain strings with no format validation anywhere in this module, so a future caller deriving either from agent/LLM-influenced content (not strictly `nextDecisionId()`'s output or a server clock) could inject a `|` to shift columns or a `\n` to forge an extra `| D-999 | ... |` row — the exact attack the pre-existing rationale-injection test covered, but id/date were untested and unprotected. Fixed by applying `escapeCell` to all five cells uniformly. Added a regression test (`ledger.test.ts`: "a pipe or newline injected via id/date cannot shift columns or forge an extra row") injecting both a `|` via id and a `\n`+forged-row via date, asserting no `| D-999` row appears and both cells escape correctly.
