2026-07-19 W5-04 done — Blueprint stage (Phase 2.5) + FR-P7 decision-complete gate, restored from the sound `blocked/w5-04` implementation (commits 15561fc/2674faa) plus the conductor's HIGH marker-injection fix. `packages/pipeline/src/blueprint/**`: `synth.ts`'s `synthesizeBlueprint` assembles a v1 blueprint (condensed sections + one `FOUNDER-DECISION` marker per open question, reusing `decisions/founder-slate.ts`'s `buildFounderSlate` rather than reimplementing slate validation); `revision.ts`'s `resolveOpenQuestion` runs one revision cycle (single-line UNRESOLVED->RESOLVED marker replace + version bump, every other line preserved byte-for-byte); `gate.ts`'s `decideBlueprintUnlock` is the FR-P7 validator for phases 3-4 — it re-parses raw blueprint markdown on every call (never trusts a cached/claimed "decision-complete" flag) and cross-checks every RESOLVED marker's cited D-ID against the real ledger via `decisions/ledger.ts`'s `citesDecisionId`, refusing a marker citing a D-ID absent from `docs/DECISIONS.md` as a spoofed resolution (gate.test.ts's headline RED FIXTURE). Marker-injection fix (`markers.ts`, the reason the prior attempt stayed blocked): `formatUnresolvedMarkerLine`/`formatResolvedMarkerLine` interpolated `question`/`decisionSummary`/`decisionId` into a marker line raw; since `parseMarkers` matches each physical line against a non-anchored, non-global `STRICT_MARKER_RE` (leftmost match wins), an embedded newline in any of the three would split into a genuine second physical line, and a value containing a full `<!-- FOUNDER-DECISION: ... -->` sequence would be matched *instead of* the real trailing marker on the same line — silently dropping the real key from `parseMarkers`' output while forging an attacker-chosen key/status/D-ID (a live FR-P7 gate bypass: a founder's real open question could vanish from the unresolved list). Fixed with a single `sanitizeMarkerText` helper applied uniformly to all three interpolated values: strips `\r\n|\r|\n` and both comment-delimiter tokens (`<!--`, `-->`) before the line is built, so no embedded value can ever complete a marker-shaped comment or introduce a new physical line. For `question`/`decisionSummary` (free-text prose outside the marker comment) this always yields a clean, well-formed marker for the real key; a corrupted `decisionId` (embedded *inside* the comment) can't forge anything either — the leftover text just fails `STRICT_MARKER_RE`'s `(D-\d+)? -->` tail, which `parseMarkers` already reports as `malformed`, its documented fail-closed path (blocks the gate exactly like a genuine unresolved marker, never silently ignored). 3 new RED FIXTURE tests in `markers.test.ts`: an evil `question` with an embedded newline + forged marker collapses to exactly one well-formed line whose `parseMarkers` result names only the real key/status, never the forged one; an evil `decisionSummary` with the same payload likewise yields exactly one marker (the real key, resolved by the real D-ID); an evil `decisionId` with the same payload yields one physical line that fails closed to `malformed`, never a resolved/forged key. Gate: `pnpm lint && pnpm typecheck && pnpm test` = 2189 passed | 1 skipped (284 test files) workspace-wide. `node scripts/validate-plan.mjs`/`validate-traceability.mjs` = OK (pre-existing gaps unrelated to W5-04/FR-P7).
2026-07-19 W5-04 gate-fix — conductor's HIGH finding fixed: `sanitizeMarkerText` (packages/pipeline/src/blueprint/markers.ts) stripped the delimiter *tokens* (`<!--`, `-->`) in a single non-recursive `.replace(/<!--|-->/g, '')` pass, which is bypassable by split-token concatenation — an attacker-controlled value interleaving fragments around real (matched-and-removed) copies of the *other* token reassembles into an intact forged delimiter after one pass (verified with node: `"<-->!--" + "..." + "--<!-->"` sanitizes to the exact intact `"<!--...-->"`), letting a forged marker shadow/drop the real trailing marker via `STRICT_MARKER_RE`'s leftmost-match, non-global exec. Fixed by switching from token-level to character-level stripping: `sanitizeMarkerText` now deletes every occurrence of the individual characters `<`, `!`, `>` (plus the existing newline normalization), so no `<!--` or `-->` can ever be reassembled from fragments regardless of how the original value interleaves real/partial tokens — no matched-and-removed substring can leave behind a completable fragment when the completing characters themselves are gone. Added a regression test (`markers.test.ts`: "split-token delimiter reassembly cannot forge a marker via single-pass token stripping") using the exact split-token payload from the gate finding, asserting the forged delimiter never appears in the output and `parseMarkers` reports only the real key/status. Gate: `pnpm lint && pnpm typecheck && pnpm test` = 2190 passed | 1 skipped (284 test files) workspace-wide.

SELF-ATTEST FIX (CRITICAL, the point of this ticket): `pipeline-routes/request-body.ts`'s `RunPipelineRequestBody`/`parseRequestBody` has no `ledgerMarkdown` field at all — it is never read from the POST body, in any form. `pipeline-routes/ledger.ts`'s new `readLedgerMarkdown` derives it server-side from the project's own real, on-disk `docs/DECISIONS.md` (matching `packages/pipeline/src/run/types.ts`'s own doc comment for what this value means), defaulting to `''` for a fresh project with no decisions yet — real persisted state an untrusted agent session posting this body cannot influence. CONCURRENCY FIX (the second HIGH a prior attempt was blocked on): `decideSlate` is a non-async function so the type system forbids an `await` in its critical section — read-ledger -> compute-D-ID -> write-ledger (`ledger-file.ts`'s sync `readFileSync`/`writeFileSync`, not `fs.promises`) -> guarded DB UPDATE (`... WHERE id = ? AND status = 'open'`, `result.changes !== 1` backstop) all run as one synchronous JS call, so a concurrent decide on a different slate can never interleave mid-way and race the same ledger snapshot; the ledger file is only written after the guarded UPDATE succeeds, so a losing racer never touches the file. `ledger-file.ts`'s `appendDecisionToLedger` seeds the standard header+table for a project's very first decision (`@dokima/pipeline`'s `appendDecision` deliberately refuses when there's no existing `| D-<n> |` row to anchor after). Routes: `POST/GET /api/v1/projects/:id/slates` (create/list, `?status=open|decided`), `POST /api/v1/slates/:id/decide?project=` (`?project=` query-param disambiguator, same shape as `board-project.ts`'s verb routes), `GET /api/v1/projects/:id/decisions` (the D-ID ledger, decided-only). Intentionally NOT wired into `apps/server/src/api/server.ts`'s bootstrap (composition root, outside this ticket's `apps/server/src/api/decisions/**` write_scope) — same precedent as W5-18's `registerPipelineRoutes`, exported and Fastify-inject-tested in isolation, wiring left as a follow-up. 16 new tests (11 `store.test.ts`, including the red regression: a ledger row whose free-text contains "D-999" still yields the correct sequential next ID, routed through the real `decideSlate` path rather than testing `nextDecisionId` directly; 5 `routes.test.ts` covering the create/list/decide/ledger round-trip plus 404/409/400 refusals). 2026-07-20 W7-03 gate-fix — dedupe.ts's group key (`${kind} ${ticketId ?? ''} ${content}`) was naive space-joined concatenation, not injective: a space landing on a field boundary could collide two distinct (ticketId, content) pairs onto one key (e.g. ticketId='a', content='b c' vs ticketId='a b', content='c'), silently decaying a semantically distinct fact — confirmed by direct reproduction. Fixed by building the key via `JSON.stringify([kind, ticketId ?? null, normalizedContent])`. Also fixed unrelated file corruption found while patching: two stray NUL bytes (0x00) sat in place of ASCII spaces on that line, tripping up grep/text tooling; corrected at the byte level, rest of the file/package verified NUL-free. 2026-07-20 W7-05 gate-fix — the prior "done" pass shipped a triage flow that was unreachable in production: `routes.ts` hardcoded both `filedBy` (on file) and `triagedBy` (on triage) to the single v1 `OPERATOR_ACTOR_ID`, so `loadPendingReport` (`triage.ts`) always saw `report.filedBy === triagedBy` and threw `SelfTriageError` for every report filed through the shipped "File field report" entry points — accept-to-playbook/accept-to-ticket/reject were only exercised in tests via a helper that bypassed the REST route to plant a fabricated non-operator `filedBy` directly in the DB. The D-020 SELF_ACCEPT precedent this borrowed from doesn't naturally transfer here (ticket claim/close is a real agent identity distinct from the human who accepts; field-report filing and triage both collapsed onto the operator). Fix keeps the `SelfTriageError` refusal (removing it would let the operator file-then-self-confirm a report as `verifiedBy: 'challenger'` into the playbook — a real Law 4/5 breach `triage.ts`'s own docstring warns against) and instead makes `filedBy` honest: new `apps/server/src/api/lessons/resolve-filed-by.ts` (`resolveFiledBy`, split out of `routes.ts` to stay under the 400-line file-size cap) re-reads the exact logged event a `trace`/`escalation` `sourceRef` (`prefill.ts`'s `trace:<runId>:<seq>` / `escalation:<ticketId>:<occurredAt>` shapes) points at and attributes the report to *that event's real actor* (typically a berth/agent identity — `berths.ts` claims/starts tickets under a real machine actor, never `operator`) instead of trusting any client-supplied claim. This is what makes D-020 actually apply: a report filed from a session-trace row or escalation card for a ticket a berth is working now resolves `filedBy` to that berth's actor, genuinely distinct from the operator who triages, so triage is reachable end-to-end through the real entry points. A `source: 'manual'` report (or a `sourceRef` that doesn't resolve, e.g. stale/malformed) still falls back to `filedBy: OPERATOR_ACTOR_ID` and stays legitimately un-self-triageable — a human confirming their own manually-typed report, same refusal as before, now correctly scoped instead of universal. 10 new tests (`resolve-filed-by.test.ts`: 9 covering trace/escalation match, the `unknown`-runId fallback, malformed refs, non-matching ticket/timestamp, non-`escalation.`-prefixed events at the same ticket+timestamp; `routes.test.ts`: 3 new REST-level cases — trace-resolved, escalation-resolved, and the sourceRef-doesn't-resolve fallback — replacing the prior test's now-inaccurate "every report filed through this API is necessarily filedBy: operator" framing). 2026-07-21 W8-04 gate-fix (retry) — Board export/import bundle: conductor flagged a CRITICAL — `importExportBundle` inserted receipts without verifying their cryptographic anchor. A receipt row is not self-authenticating (`packages/events/migrations/002_receipts.sql` has no signature column); authenticity rests entirely on a matching `gate.receipt_minted`/`gate.waived` event whose `payload.contentMac` was HMAC-tagged with the project's secret `signingKey` (`packages/events/src/receipts.ts`'s `verifyReceipt`). `verifyChain` covers events only, never receipts, so an actor holding just the shared Bearer token (no `signingKey`) could POST a bundle with a fabricated close/gate receipt and no real anchor into an empty project and have it accepted — exactly the "flip state without a real receipt" pattern law #4 prohibits. Fix: `export-bundle-import.ts` now replays the anchor check (part 1 of `verifyReceipt`: recompute `computeReceiptMac` from `@dokima/events` over each bundle receipt's own fields using the target project's `signingKey`, require a bundle event of the kind-appropriate type carrying a matching `receiptId` plus a MAC that constant-time-equals the recomputed one) against the bundle's *own* `events` array, before any DB write; any unanchored receipt rejects the whole bundle via a new `ReceiptAnchorVerificationFailedError` (400, lists the offending ids). `importExportBundle`/`registerExportRoutes`/`ExportRoutesOptions` now take a required `signingKey` (the same keychain-resolved secret `mintReceipt`/`verifyReceipt` already require — not a new secret path). Added 2 red-fixture tests to `export-routes.test.ts`: an orphan receipt (no anchor event at all) and a receipt anchored by a syntactically valid, correctly hash-chained event whose `contentMac` was guessed rather than computed with the real `signingKey` (the "self-consistent forgery" case) — both rejected 400. The second flagged item (`hash-chain.ts`'s TODO-looking comment) was re-checked and confirmed a false positive: it's meta-commentary describing a permanent, intentional, already-tested architectural fact (`packages/shared` can't depend on `packages/events`, so the algorithm is hand-mirrored; `export-hash-parity.test.ts` proves the two copies agree byte-for-byte) — no backlog item needed, left as-is. Gate: `pnpm lint && pnpm typecheck && pnpm test` = 2592 passed | 1 skipped (362 test files) workspace-wide (+2 tests, same file count as the prior close); `node scripts/validate-plan.mjs`/`validate-traceability.mjs` = OK, same pre-existing gaps only; `validate-file-size.sh` = same 2 pre-existing hard gaps (`scripts/conductor.mjs`, `packages/events/src/receipts.ts`), largest touched file `export-bundle-import.ts` at 247 lines; `validate-circular-deps.sh` = 0 gaps.

Fix, both in `apps/server/src/bootstrap/audit-tail.ts`: `readHighWaterFile` now only swallows `ENOENT` (fresh install, nothing recorded yet) and rethrows any other error, so a corrupted or unreadable mirror file fails boot loudly instead of resetting the high-water baseline to 0; `writeHighWaterFile` now writes to a `.tmp` file in the same directory (name salted with pid + timestamp) and `fs.rename()`s it into place, so a partial write can never land at the real path. Added two red-fixture tests: `audit-tail.test.ts` seeds `audit-highwater.json` with invalid JSON and asserts `checkAndUpdateHighWater` rejects; `boot-sequence.test.ts` does the same at the full `runBootSequence` level (real boot first to establish a genuine mirror, then corrupt it, then assert the next boot throws) rather than being treated as first-boot. 2026-07-27 **W9 wave closed — board is 107/107 tickets, 388/388 points.** The last five open tickets landed in one supervised wave (W8-10 orchestrator lane; W9-02/03/05/04 ui lane, serialized per Law 1). Each ticket was implemented by an independent Sonnet session against its own `write_scope`, gated with `pnpm lint && pnpm typecheck && pnpm test`, and merged `--no-ff` by a separate orchestrator that owned `plan.json` as single writer (workers never touched the board — no claim race). Final gate on main: validate-plan OK, validate-traceability OK, lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing `docs/dogfood/run-dogfood.mjs` warning), typecheck clean, **391 test files / 2738 passed | 1 skipped**, and **e2e 58/58**. Suite grew 2726 → 2738; every increment matches the tests its ticket added (+1 W8-10, +2 W9-02, +2 W9-05, +3 W9-03, +4 W9-04).

- **W8-10** — real defect, red fixture first: `proposePlanItemsFromOnboardFindings` deduped only against persisted rows, so two findings sharing a `catalogIdFor` both passed the check and collided on `plan_items.id`'s unique constraint. The fixture reproduced `UNIQUE constraint failed: plan_items.id` on unmodified code before the fix; duplicates now collapse with higher severity winning and recommendation evidence merged.
- **W9-02** — two candidate causes were **refuted with repro tests** (the empty-receipts branch already rendered correctly; the fetch did fire) before the real one was confirmed: `fetchReceipts().then(setReceipts)` had no `.catch`, so a rejected fetch left `receipts` at `null` and the panel sat on "Loading receipts…" forever — a C-1 violation. Now surfaces an error alert; empty state covered by test.
- **W9-05** — the pane already had `overflow-x: auto` and correct a11y markup; the defect was that Chromium reserves zero layout space for overlay scrollbars, so overflow was invisible (446px visible vs 1192px scrollWidth at 1440x900). Adds a 4-layer scroll-shadow that fades only the edge still scrollable. Self-review caught an invalid `background-image` declaration (a bare position inside the image list computes the property to `none`, silently — jsdom could not see it) before it shipped; the regression test reproduces that exact bug.
- **W9-03** — sample chat threads replayed realistic cost chips and model attributions indistinguishable from real telemetry. Adds a visible "Sample" badge; chips are deliberately kept and only their context labelled. Additive testid only.
- **W9-04** — CSS-only, zero JSX: one `button` base rule adopts the header's `.theme-toggle` pill. Element specificity (0-0-1) sits below every class selector, so ~15 already-styled controls are untouched while ~100 raw natives inherit it; six overrides stop the base leaking into controls that intentionally reset button chrome. DOM contracts hold by construction.

**Two process defects hit during this wave, both worth keeping.** (1) **Node**: the repo is pinned to Node 22 (`.nvmrc`, `engines`), but `fnm`'s default is Node 24; running the suite under 24 fails ~50 `apps/server` tests with a `better-sqlite3` `NODE_MODULE_VERSION 127 vs 137` mismatch that reads exactly like catastrophic breakage. Nothing was broken. Any gate command here must put v22.23.1 on PATH first. (2) **Mis-merge**: a ticket agent working in the main checkout left it on `sw/w9-05`; `git merge sw/w9-05` run from that branch is a silent no-op that still exits 0, so a "merge" was reported green while W9-05 stayed unmerged and its board commit landed on the wrong branch. Caught only because the next agent's report opened with "the repo was NOT clean-on-main as stated" and it branched off `main` itself. Merges are now guarded by asserting `HEAD` and verifying with `git branch --contains` rather than trusting an exit code. Related: agent worktrees were created at `.claude/worktrees/`, which is **not** gitignored, so a sibling agent's `pnpm lint` picked up a second full checkout and reported 11 phantom errors — worth gitignoring `.claude/` or relocating worktrees outside the repo.

2026-07-27 (2nd) **Dogfood findings triage — the 11 CRITICALs are dismissed, and the triage found something bigger than the claim.** The W8-01 run's 55 findings collapse to ~22 distinct claims; the inflation is exactly the missing in-batch dedup that W8-10 fixed earlier today (each specialist's copy of one finding was filed separately). All **11 CRITICAL findings are a single claim** — "export bundle import does not verify receipt signatures" — reported independently by landscape-mapper, entry-point-tracer, db-architect, component-mapper, sdlc-lead ×2, semgrep-runner, owasp-llm-checker, cloud-security-checker, attack-chainer and threat-modeler. Repetition by 11 agents is not corroboration; they read the same code with the same blind spot.

**VERDICT: false positive.** `002_receipts.sql` has no signature/HMAC column by design — authenticity is the anchoring `gate.receipt_minted`/`gate.waived` event's `contentMac`, recomputed over `id, kind, projectId, phase, ticketId, validators, inputTreeHash, verifyCommand, verifyExit, signedBy` (`receipts.ts:173-188`). So "verify the signature" asks for something that does not exist. A pinning test now proves the guarantee that makes this a false positive: a bundle whose receipt content is edited while its anchoring MAC is left at the pre-edit value is **rejected 400** — the MAC covers every content field, not merely presence. `verifyReceipt`'s checks 2/3 (input-tree and validator currency) are correctly N/A during a raw history restore.

**One real delta, correctly scoped as LOW.** Import replays check 1 only; check 4 (waiver FR-P2 signer re-check) is not replayed, and a bundle pairing a genuine waiver receipt with a tampered `identities[]` entry is accepted 201. It cannot manufacture a usable agent-signed waiver: `signedBy` is inside the MAC, `mintReceipt` enforces FR-P2 at mint time, and identities are UPDATE/DELETE-blocked (`001_init.sql:12-26`) — so tampering can only make the imported state more obviously invalid, and a full `verifyReceipt` against the target DB still fails it. Both behaviours are now pinned by test.

**The larger finding, which none of the 11 mentioned: the route is not reachable.** `registerExportRoutes` has zero production call sites — only its own definition and tests (verified by grep). `export-routes.ts:10-19` documents this as a deliberate write_scope artifact with precedent (`decisions/routes.ts` W5-13 and `pipeline-routes` W5-18 were both dark until W5-22 wired them) and calls wiring "a one-line follow-up." That follow-up never happened, so **ROADMAP's W8 exit criterion "export/import round-trips with chain verification" is met at module level but not in the running product.** The same is true of `decideAdvance` (phase-gate advancement, FR-P1/P2): zero production call sites, so the point-of-use safety net for the LOW gap above is itself unwired. Neither is a hidden defect — both are documented build-then-wire seams — but both are unfinished.

**Also disproved without triage:** "hard-coded npm registry token in `.npmrc`" (the file contains one comment block and `ignore-scripts=true`, an intentional SC-16 control — no token exists) and "lockfile not committed" (`pnpm-lock.yaml` is tracked). **Still untriaged:** the ×11 "decision ledger writes are not atomic across concurrent requests" HIGH cluster, and a ×10 MEDIUM cluster about auth-hook bypass for routes registered outside the decisions module. Noted as orthogonal-but-real by the triage: neither `importExportBundle` nor `verifyReceipt` compares `bundle.projectId` against the target project, so a bundle from project A can be replayed verbatim into an empty project B.

2026-07-27 (3rd) **Auth-bypass cluster triaged — the claim is backwards, and it resolves the export-routes wiring question.** The dogfood's 10 MEDIUM findings ("authentication enforcement can be bypassed for routes added outside the decisions module", from landscape-mapper, entry-point-tracer, component-mapper, sdlc-lead ×2, semgrep-runner, owasp-llm-checker, cloud-security-checker, attack-chainer, threat-modeler) are **one claim, and it inverts the actual design**.

**VERDICT: false positive.** Auth is enforced *globally*: `registerAuthHook` adds an `onRequest` hook straight to the root Fastify instance (`auth-plugin.ts:110-127`) at `server.ts:83`, before the first route registers at line 85. The codebase contains **zero** `app.register()` calls in production code — no encapsulation boundary exists for a route to hide behind — so the hook necessarily covers every route added afterwards, including future ones. What misled all 10 specialists is the reverse of their conclusion: `decisions/routes.ts` and `export-routes.ts` carry **redundant** per-route `preHandler`s (they predate being wired in), and the specialists read those as evidence that other modules were unprotected. Empirically confirmed: 16 routes spanning every module, hit with no `Authorization` header, all returned 401 `rule: 'D-005'`.

**The recommended fix is worse than the non-problem.** "Enforce global authentication registration in server.ts" is already true, so read literally it is a no-op; read as "wrap routes via `.register()`/`fastify-plugin`" it would *introduce* the encapsulation boundary that currently doesn't exist, and risks swallowing `requiresToken()`'s deliberate path carve-outs — i.e. **it could break `/healthz`**. Related dismissal: the companion "`/healthz` lacks authentication" finding is the same intentional design seen from the other side — the bearer token is exempted per D-005/API_DESIGN.md §1, but the SC-08 host/origin check still applies (`server.test.ts:174-184` proves an evil Host is still 403). No `/readyz` route exists in this codebase at all.

**Real but narrow (the kernel of truth):** nothing *mechanically* asserts auth coverage — no route-enumerating test, no lint rule (verified absent from `eslint.config.js` and `scripts/validators`). The invariant "every route is registered on the single root `app`" holds by convention, not by an enforced guard. Suggested follow-up is a small vitest asserting every non-exempt path 401s without a token — **not** the findings' proposed global-registration rewrite.

**This resolves the pending wiring decision.** `export-routes.ts:17`'s self-defence — "every route runs its own auth preHandler, so these routes are self-protecting regardless of whether server.ts registers them" — was checked rather than taken at its word, and it holds: `{ preHandler: auth }` at lines 165/184 running the same `checkAuth()`, and `export-routes.test.ts:40-51` boots them on a **bare Fastify instance with no global hook at all** and still gets a 401. That is proof in exactly the unwired state. Wiring the routes into `server.ts` would therefore only add redundant global coverage on top — safe, and it closes ROADMAP's W8 exit criterion in the product rather than only at module level. Still an owner decision because it changes what the shipped server exposes.

2026-07-27 (4th) **Export/import routes wired — ROADMAP W8's exit criterion now holds in the product, not just at module level.** `registerExportRoutes` previously had zero production call sites, so "export/import round-trips with chain verification" was satisfied only by standalone inject tests. Now registered in `server.ts` (2745 tests, e2e 58/58).

**It was NOT the "one-line follow-up" its own header comment promised, and the reason is worth keeping.** `ExportRoutesOptions.signingKey` is *required* — `POST /import` replays every receipt's anchor MAC through `computeReceiptMac(content, signingKey)` — and `server.ts` had **no signing-key resolution at all**; the only one in the codebase was the CLI's (`run-cmd.ts:173`, `--signing-key` ?? `DOKIMA_SIGNING_KEY`). The secret had to be threaded through `BuildApiServerOptions` and `main.ts` first. The estimate was written from the route's side and missed the caller's missing dependency; the header comment now records that it was wrong rather than quietly deleting the claim.

**Registration is deliberately conditional and fail-closed.** No signing key ⇒ the two routes are not registered at all. The tempting shortcut — `signingKey: process.env.DOKIMA_SIGNING_KEY ?? ''` — would have been a real vulnerability: with an empty key an attacker holding only the Bearer token can compute matching `contentMac` values and plant receipts, defeating the exact anchor check the earlier triage proved was load-bearing. A missing endpoint is safe; an unverifiable one is not. `main.ts` reads the same env var the CLI already uses, so there is one convention, not two.

**Both branches are pinned by test**, and the positive one was verified to fail when the wiring is removed (checked by actually removing it) rather than merely asserted: with a key, `GET /export` 401s unauthenticated and, authenticated, reaches its own project lookup (404 "no project registered") rather than Fastify's route-miss 404; without a key, an *authenticated* `POST /import` gets a route-miss 404 that does not mention a project. That distinction is what separates "route absent" from "route present but unhappy".

**Prerequisite for operators:** the export/import endpoints only appear when `DOKIMA_SIGNING_KEY` is set in the server's environment. Silent absence is intentional, but it does mean a deployment expecting these routes must configure the key — worth a line in DEPLOYMENT.md when someone next touches it.

2026-07-27 (5th) **`decideAdvance` NOT wired — and correcting this file's own earlier claim about it.** Entry (3rd) above called `decideAdvance` an "inert build-then-wire seam", the same category as `registerExportRoutes`. That was wrong, and the difference matters. Export was a finished route missing a caller. `decideAdvance` is a **consumer whose producer deliberately does not exist**, with no caller either. Three facts, each verified by grep rather than inferred:

1. **No phase-gate receipt producer.** `decideAdvance` requires `input.gateReceiptId` — "the phase's own gate receipt, if a validator run has ever completed for it" — and re-verifies it against `PHASES[n].validators`. The function that would mint one, `mintValidatorRunReceipt`, has **zero production callers** (only an `index.ts` re-export and a doc-comment mention in `advance.ts`). The single production minter is `packages/harbormaster/src/loop-gates.ts:306`, which mints `kind: 'close'` for a **ticket**, not a phase gate.
2. **Its absence is deliberate, not an oversight.** `pipeline-routes/index.ts` records a **SECURITY_W5 CRITICAL FIX**: every `PipelineRunEvent` is appended as a plain hash-chained audit event and is explicitly *not* anchored by a gate receipt, because "no independent validator runs on this model-authored phase output, so minting a 'passing' gate receipt for it would be self-attestation (Law 4/5)."
3. **No phase-advance flow exists** anywhere in `apps/server` — no route, verb, or state machine. Every "advance" hit is unrelated (event-log cursors, high-water marks, escalation rungs).

So there is no safe wiring available today. `gateReceiptId: null` yields a gate that refuses every advance — a guardrail that blocks everything is not a guardrail. Minting phase gate receipts from `runPipeline` to satisfy it would reintroduce exactly the self-attestation the W5 CRITICAL fix removed, trading a dormant seam for a live Law 4/5 regression. **The honest status is not "unwired" but "blocked on an unbuilt producer."**

The real path is a feature, not a wiring task: an independent phase-validator runner that executes `PHASES[n].validators` for real and mints a genuine receipt under a **distinct verifier identity** (Law 5, maker ≠ verifier), plus a phase-advance route to call `decideAdvance` from. Until that exists, `decideAdvance` staying dark is the correct state, and `advance.test.ts` keeps its logic pinned. Worth a W10 ticket pair rather than a follow-up line.

2026-07-27 (6th) **W9-06 + W9-07 filed — the real path for `decideAdvance`, scoped as tickets rather than a follow-up line.** Board 107 → **109 tickets / 396 pts**; W9 is 5/7 with **W9-06 claimable and W9-07 gated behind it**. Filed as W9-xx, not W10: `validate-plan.mjs` P3 pins ticket ids to `/^W\d-\d{2}[a-c]?$/`, so a two-digit wave is invalid in this repo — worth knowing before anyone proposes "W10" again.

- **W9-06 (5 pts, pipeline lane) — build the missing producer.** A phase-gate runner that executes `PHASES[n].validators` for real against on-disk deliverables and mints a genuine receipt through `mintValidatorRunReceipt`, whose **first production caller this will be**. Two constraints carry the ticket's weight: the receipt must be minted under a verifier identity *distinct* from the one that authored the phase output (Law 5, proven by a red fixture that refuses a same-identity mint), and it must NOT anchor model-authored `PipelineRunEvent` output — that path stays a plain audit event, because the SECURITY_W5 CRITICAL fix removed gate receipts there precisely to kill self-attestation. A test pins that those events stay unanchored, so the fix cannot be undone by accident while satisfying this ticket.
- **W9-07 (3 pts, depends on W9-06) — wire the consumer.** A phase-advance route calling `decideAdvance` with the real `verifyReceipt` bound, the from-phase's *current* validator set for the gate receipt and `[]` for a waiver. Refusals must carry `AdvanceResult.reasons` through to the caller rather than collapsing to a bare 403 — FR-P3's "the UI states which receipts became stale and why" is the acceptance bar. FR-P2's two-way check is demonstrated end to end (edit a deliverable → refused on input-hash mismatch; grow the validator set → refused on drift), and the waiver bypass is bounded by FR-G5 (still refused on phases 4–5) and US-407's human-signer check.

Both are `lane: pipeline` and their write scopes are disjoint (`phase-gate/**` vs `pipeline-routes/**`), so the dependency — not the lane law — is what serializes them. `advance.test.ts` already pins `decideAdvance`'s logic and needs no change: W9-07 is a caller, not a rewrite of the decision.

2026-07-27 (7th) **W9-06 returned BLOCKED on first attempt — correctly — and the fault was the ticket, not the agent.** The write_scope I filed (`apps/server/src/api/pipeline/phase-gate/**` only) made the acceptance criteria structurally unreachable. Two imports the criteria *require* do not resolve from there, proven with a probe written inside the granted scope and then deleted (worktree left clean):

```
TS2305: Module '"@dokima/pipeline"' has no exported member 'PHASES'.
TS2307: Cannot find module '@dokima/validators' or its corresponding type declarations.
```

`packages/pipeline/src/index.ts` exports only plans/interview/blueprint/decisions/decompose/run — `PHASES` is reached solely via relative imports *inside* that package. `apps/server/package.json` declares no `@dokima/validators`, and `@dokima/harbormaster`'s barrel offers no transitive path. Both independently re-verified before amending the board. This is the same wall `packages/pipeline/src/phases/types.ts` documents and that `artifacts-routes/shared.ts:21-29` worked around by freezing a local `PHASES` mirror — a workaround criterion 1 explicitly forbids here ("read live off the topology, never frozen").

**The agent rejected two dodges, and was right on both.** A ports/adapters seam would have typechecked while satisfying neither criterion: `mintValidatorRunReceipt` would still have zero production callers, and the validator set would come from an injected fake rather than the live topology. Adding the dependency to the *root* `package.json` would have resolved — root is in the conductor's `alwaysOk` allowlist — but would make `@dokima/validators` ambiently importable from every workspace package, defeating the per-package dependency discipline every other package follows. Reporting BLOCKED with a two-line handback beat both.

**Scope corrected** to include `apps/server/package.json`, `packages/pipeline/src/index.ts` and `pnpm-lock.yaml`, with the wiring folded into acceptance criterion 2 so it is visible work rather than an incidental edit. The barrel export was validated mechanically, not by reading names: `pnpm --filter @dokima/pipeline typecheck` passes with `export * from './phases/index.js'` applied, so `LockedPhaseId`/`PhaseId` do not collide.

**Process lesson, filed against myself:** the ticket was scoped without checking that its required imports resolve from its write_scope. Reachability is cheap to verify when writing a scope and expensive to discover when an agent hits the wall — a 5-point ticket bounced on a two-line dependency gap. Worth a validator: for each ticket, confirm the modules its acceptance names are importable from at least one path in its scope.

2026-07-27 (8th) **W9-06 landed — and found the reason W9-07 cannot proceed yet.** Board 110 tickets / 399 pts; W9 6/8, **claimable is now W9-08, not W9-07**.

`apps/server/src/api/pipeline/phase-gate/` (9 files) reads `getPhase(id).validators` live off the topology every call, runs them for real via `loadValidatorPack`/`runValidatorPack` against the project root, and mints through `mintValidatorRunReceipt` — **its first production caller** — only when every validator exits 0. Receipt input contents are always read off the project root and never accepted as a parameter, closing a self-attestation seam a caller-supplied-content API would have opened. Prerequisite wiring landed with it: `@dokima/validators` added to `apps/server`, and `export * from './phases/index.js'` added to the pipeline barrel.

**Maker != verifier is mechanical, verified independently.** `assertVerifierDistinctFromAuthor` throws on a blank author id — so the guard cannot be made vacuous by omission, which is the subtler failure — and throws unconditionally on a match. Call ordering checked directly: the guard is `runner.ts:122`, `runValidatorPack` is 149, the mint is 161, so a same-identity call never even spawns a validator. No override path.

**Guards proven by mutation, not assertion.** The implementer disclosed a process deviation — it built before writing fixtures, contrary to the brief — and rather than presenting fixtures-first fiction it broke each guard in turn and captured the literal failures: the identity guard neutered (4 tests red), exitCode-2 coercion allowed (`expected true to be false`), and the SECURITY_W5 mint reinstated inside `emitPhaseEvent` (`expected 4 to be +0` on the receipts count). The disclosure is the right call and the mutation evidence is arguably stronger than a fixtures-first chronology, since it proves each guard is load-bearing rather than merely present. Gate: 396 files / 2761 passed | 1 skipped, e2e 58/58.

**W9-08 filed (3 pts) — and W9-07 now depends on it.** Two defects surfaced by the build, both outside its scope, both re-verified here before filing:
1. **No phase can gate cleanly today.** `validate-mermaid.sh` exits 0 with **zero-byte stdout** (run against a clean fixture to confirm); it is the only validator that never emits an envelope — its sole `validator_exit` mention is a comment at line 334. `parseValidatorOutput('')` returns null (contract.ts:34) and `run.ts:160` normalizes that to `exitCode: 2`. Since `PHASES[0..5]` all require it (R-H3), **an ALLOWED `decideAdvance` cannot be demonstrated against real content** — which is precisely W9-07's acceptance. The ticket prefers fixing the script over loosening the contract: treating empty stdout as "clean" would weaken fail-closed for every validator.
2. **Single-finding NDJSON is misreported as malformed.** `parseValidatorOutput` tries `JSON.parse` on the whole trimmed stdout first; exactly one finding line IS valid JSON, so it parses, falls through the array and `gaps`-number branches, and returns null at contract.ts:55 → `exitCode: 2`. Two or more lines work only by accident, because concatenated lines throw into the NDJSON fallback. Independent of mermaid; hits any single-finding validator.

2026-07-28 **W9-08 complete but BLOCKED on re-signing — the work is done, the signature is not.** Branch `sw/w9-08` (`75f90c4`) fixes both defects and is green on lint, typecheck and e2e. It is NOT merged, because it cannot be merged green by anyone without the signing key.

**Why:** `content/validators/**` is a **signed content pack**. `content/manifest.json` records `validate-mermaid.sh` as `sha256:8dc78a15…`; the fixed script hashes to `bcbe842a…`, so `verifyPack()` rejects it with `file-hash-mismatch` and 5 `apps/server` pack-integrity tests fail (`doctor.test.ts` ×3, `bootstrap/cli.test.ts`, `bootstrap/packs-update.test.ts`) — reproduced here, not taken on trust. Note the Ed25519 **manifest signature itself stays valid**: nothing touched the manifest, so only the per-file hash comparison fails. **Unblock is one command by whoever holds the key:** `DOKIMA_SIGNING_KEY=… node scripts/sign-content.mjs`, then commit `content/manifest.json` (now added to W9-08's write_scope). The key is deliberately never committed and is absent from this environment, so this is genuinely not automatable here.

**The fixes themselves.** Defect 1: `validate-mermaid.sh` now sources `_lib.sh` and uses `validator_init`/`gap`/`warn`/`validator_exit` (modelled on `validate-no-ascii-art.sh`), so a clean run emits `{"validator":"validate-mermaid","gaps":0,"exit":0,"items":[]}` instead of zero bytes. Errors call `gap()`, the two warnings (M004/M010) call `warn()` — preserving the pre-existing "warnings never fail the gate" behaviour. Defect 2: `contract.ts`'s final `return null` fallthrough now delegates to the existing `parseNdjson` path, so line count no longer decides what counts as a finding; red fixture confirmed failing first (`expected null not to be null`).

**Two things worth keeping from how it was verified.** (1) The implementer **differential-tested its rewrite against the unmodified original** on a 12-check fixture and caught a false positive it had just introduced: a byte-level transcription accident had replaced a literal U+00A0 with a plain space in the non-breaking-space check (M009), making it match almost any line. Original reported 2×M013; the rewrite reported 2×M009 + 2×M013. Fixed with a byte-level patch rather than a text edit, precisely to avoid repeating the transcription error. A rewrite of a validator is exactly the case where "tests pass" is insufficient — the question is whether it reports the *same* findings, and only a differential test answers that. (2) Fail-closed was checked one layer up, not assumed: for the one pre-existing expectation that had to be loosened (`{"status":"ok"}`), `run.ts:176-178`'s `declaresClean`/`declaresGaps` agreement check still forces `contract-violation` → `exitCode: 2`, so the loosening cannot produce a silent pass.

**Scoping lesson, third instance this session and the sharpest.** W9-06 bounced on unreachable imports; W9-08's criterion 2 was caught pre-dispatch naming an out-of-scope module; this one is a dependency **no import-reachability validator would have caught**, because the missing target is a *signature*, not a symbol. Rule for this repo: any ticket touching `content/**` must include `content/manifest.json` in its write_scope and budget a re-sign, or it cannot land.

2026-07-28 (2nd) **W9-07 landed — the phase gate is closed in code. Board 109/110; the only open ticket is W9-08, blocked on a re-sign.** `POST /api/v1/projects/:id/phases/:n/advance` binds `AdvanceDeps.verifyReceipt` to the real `verifyReceipt` over a read-only reader handle, the phase's current on-disk content (read fresh via the same helper W9-06's runner uses — never caller-POSTed) and the keychain-resolved signing key. `decideAdvance` itself untouched. Gate: 397 files / 2775 passed | 1 skipped, e2e 58/58.

**`reasons` survives to the caller on both paths** — verbatim onto the 200 and the 422 alike. That was the single easiest thing to lose (FR-P3: "the UI states which receipts became stale and why"), and it was checked in the merged code, not taken from the report.

**Seven cases, each with its own test:** no gate receipt; FR-P2 input-hash mismatch after editing a doc on disk; FR-P2 validator-set drift; human-signed waiver rescues (`waived: true`); agent-signed waiver rejected; **blocklisted human name rejected** (US-407 AC-1's second guard); clean human waiver still refused on phase 4 (FR-G5). Red-fixture-first was proven by stashing the implementation — all 11 route-hitting tests failed 404 before it existed.

**A near-miss worth recording.** The implementer's first agent-signed-waiver test asserted `reasons.some(r => r.includes('FR-P2'))` — but *every* waiver-branch reason contains that string, so the assertion would have passed even with the signer-kind guard completely broken. It was tightened to `verifyReceipt`'s exact wording and the missing blocklisted-name case was added. This is the same class as W9-05's invalid CSS and W9-08's U+00A0 transcription slip: **a test that cannot fail is worse than no test, because it reports coverage it does not have.** Substring assertions on shared prefixes are a reliable way to produce one.

**Disclosed caveat, deliberately not engineered around.** Every phase requires `validate-mermaid`; the real script emits zero-byte stdout, normalizing to `exitCode: 2`, so **this route refuses every real advance in production until W9-08 lands**. No `exitCode === 2` bypass was added — verified in the merged source, where both mentions of it are the comment explaining why one must not exist. A permanently-refusing gate that looks green is the exact failure this arc set out to prevent.

**Correction recorded against my own earlier call:** W9-08 was briefly filed as a hard `depends_on` for W9-07, on the reading that an allowed advance could not be demonstrated until the real validator was fixed. That overstated it — W9-06's `runner.test.ts:63` already mints a clean receipt from a fixture validator, and W9-07's criteria never required real content. W9-08 blocks *production* usability, not the build. Propagating a subagent's framing without testing it against the ticket's own text cost a cycle.

2026-07-28 (3rd) **W9-09/10/11 landed — the conductor is now genuinely repo-agnostic, and guarded.** Board 112 done / 1 blocked (W9-08); 399 files / 2807 tests / e2e 58/58. These three were filed after attempting EXECUTION_ORDER.md Phase B1 (conductor on Kryptkeeper) surfaced that "point it at another repo" was not a config-writing task.

- **W9-09** — `scripts/**` was covered by NEITHER lint nor vitest. eslint ignored it, hiding **11 real errors** (a control-character regex and 9 empty `catch {}` blocks); `vitest.workspace.ts` listed only `apps/*|packages/*`, so a test placed there was *silently never collected* — not failing, never running. All 11 fixed with no rule widened. Pure helpers extracted to `conductor-lib.mjs`, runtime equivalence proven by byte-identical `--lint` output.
- **W9-10** — `boardPath` config key. The loader was the easy part: **three mentions live inside the AGENT PROMPT**, so fixing `planPath` alone would start the conductor cleanly and then send every agent to a board that does not exist — a silent failure that reads as an agent fault. Prompt extracted to `codingPrompt(t, feedback, boardPath)`. Also fixed the `alwaysOk` union, done-check message, lint error line, and TWO `git add` sites (the second found by grep, not named in the ticket).
- **W9-11** — board writes are byte-preserving. The old `JSON.stringify(plan,null,2)+'\n'` diverged **2,842 bytes** on the real 499KB board (906 `\uXXXX` escapes → literal UTF-8, plus a trailing newline the file lacks), churning ~400 lines per status write. The fix preserves the file's OWN convention rather than imposing one. Flipping one status now yields a 2-line diff out of ~13,600 — verified on this very commit.

**The verification lesson of the wave, three shapes of the same bug.** W9-09's break/restore found a round-trip test that stayed GREEN when `serializePlan`'s indent broke, because `JSON.parse` ignores whitespace. W9-11's found something worse: breaking `serializePlan` reds 4 tests, but reverting only the **production call site** to a bare `serializePlan(plan)` left all 28 green — a correct function that never reached production. Closed by extracting `writePlan()` as the sole write path. **Testing the unit is not testing the wiring**; both W9-05's invalid CSS and W9-07's shared-prefix substring assertion were the same family. Every one was caught by breaking the subject and confirming red — never by reading the test.

**Known fragility, recorded not hidden:** W9-11's one-line-diff test hardcodes ticket `W9-09`'s row from the live `plan.json`, a file outside its write_scope that the orchestrator mutates. It survives a status-only edit (verified: 32/32 green after closing W9-11) but will need updating if that ticket's row changes shape.

2026-07-28 (4th) **Kryptkeeper conductor dry-run PASSED — and found a fourth undeclared dependency.** `node scripts/conductor.mjs --lint` reports **0 errors, 0 warnings** against Kryptkeeper's 125-ticket board at `docs/board/plan.json`. That validates W9-10's `boardPath` work end-to-end against the real target rather than a fixture.

**Proved it read the right board rather than silently defaulting**, which is the failure this class of config change invites: pointing `boardPath` at a nonexistent file fails with `ENOENT` naming that exact file, and `loadPlanFrom` against the real path returns `125 tickets / project=kryptkeeper`. Kryptkeeper's tickets already carry `id/title/module/lane/write_scope/depends_on/acceptance/points/status` — the shape the linter wants — differing only in `wave` for `phase` plus an `frs` field, none of which the lint objects to.

**The find: `scripts/models.json`.** The first attempt crashed with `ENOENT ... scripts/models.json` — the per-role model routing table is read at module scope, so it fails at IMPORT, before `--lint` or any argument parsing. The error names a file with no indication that a config is missing. It is not covered by `conductor.config.json`, so that file's `$note` claim of holding everything project-specific remains incomplete: importing the harness actually needs THREE vendored files plus a config, and the third is discoverable only by crashing. Filed as **W9-12** (2 pts).

**This is the fourth time this session that attempting the thing beat reasoning about it** — after export-routes' required `signingKey`, W9-06's unreachable imports, and W9-08's content-pack signature. Each was invisible to inspection and obvious within seconds of execution. The dry-run cost ~15 minutes and would have surfaced during an unattended launch otherwise.

**Kryptkeeper is now conductor-ready and pushed** (`41dca92`, both remotes): harness vendored, `conductor.config.json` with `boardPath`, gates `go build ./...` + `go test ./...`. **Lint deliberately omitted** — golangci-lint is not installed there and has no config (Kryptkeeper's own W1-07), and since `CONFIG.gates` is fully replaceable a project omits what it cannot run rather than red-failing every ticket on a missing tool. Conductor runtime artifacts gitignored, mirroring this repo. **Importing the harness launches nothing** — starting an unattended run on a commercial codebase stays an explicit founder decision.

**Also pushed: `sw/w9-08`.** The branch was flagged as having a commit on no remote — a *complete, green, founder-blocked* ticket existing only on one disk. Now on both remotes at `75f90c4`. Blocked work still deserves a backup.

2026-07-28 (5th) **Dogfood findings board FULLY TRIAGED — 55 findings → 16 distinct claims → 4 real issues.** The last 20 findings (13 claims) are done; nothing on that board is now unassessed.

| Cluster | Findings | Distinct | Real |
|---|---|---|---|
| CRITICAL — export-bundle receipt signatures | 11 | 1 | 0 (false positive) |
| HIGH — decision-ledger atomicity | 11 | 1 | **1 real bug**, fixed |
| MEDIUM — auth bypass outside decisions | 10 | 1 | 0 (false positive, and backwards) |
| Stragglers (this pass) | 20 | 13 | **3 real**, all LOW |

**The one that is live, not theoretical: CI's supply-chain gate is currently RED.** `pnpm audit --prod --audit-level high` — the exact command in `.github/workflows/ci.yml:181-202` — exits **1** with 3 high production advisories: `fast-uri` host-confusion ×2 and `find-my-way` HTTP/2 DDoS, both transitive through fastify. Severity for this system is genuinely low (localhost-bound, single-operator, nothing directly chosen), but the gate is failing *now*, which is a fact about CI rather than a judgement. Fix: `pnpm update` or a `pnpm.overrides` entry, then re-run the gate command. *(Method note: a first check piped the command into `tail`, so `$?` captured tail's exit and reported 0 — the pipe swallowed the failure. Re-run unpiped to see exit 1. Third instance today of a shell reporting success for something that failed.)*

**The other two real ones, both LOW.** No `Content-Security-Policy` header anywhere — notable because `docs/TECH_STACK.md:107-111` already commits to `script-src 'self'`, making this an unmet internal commitment rather than a generic best practice; compensating controls are strong (zero `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`, `isSafeHref()` scheme allowlist, hand-rolled Mermaid SVG via real React elements). And `EXPORT_BUNDLE_VERSION = 1` is defined independently in `apps/server/src/api/export-bundle-types.ts:11` and `packages/shared/src/export/types.ts:10`, kept in sync by hand with no test cross-checking them — a mismatch would fail loudly at import validation rather than corrupt silently, but it is unenforced.

**Ten of thirteen were false positives, and four of the recommended fixes were worse than the findings.** All three healthz claims recommend adding `checkAuth` to `/healthz`, which would **break liveness monitoring**; their dependency-verification half is a no-op, since `healthz.ts:12-16` already calls `isDbOpen()` and `wsHub.isUp` and returns 503 `{status:'degraded'}` (proven live at `server.test.ts:162-172`). The CORS fix would *add* `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` headers where the app deliberately sends none and rejects cross-origin at the allowlist. The XSS fix would replace a renderer that never produces an HTML string with one that does, plus a sanitizer to keep correctly configured forever. "Missing rate limiting on `/api/v1/auth/*`" names a route that does not exist — there is no login endpoint; auth is a static per-request bearer check, not a credential-guessing surface.

**Third cluster running where the remedy was more dangerous than the finding.** Worth carrying into any future triage: assess the recommended fix as its own claim, not as a given.

2026-07-28 (6th) **CI supply-chain gate back to green.** `pnpm audit --prod --audit-level high` — the exact command in `.github/workflows/ci.yml:181-202` — now exits **0** ("No known vulnerabilities found"), down from exit 1 with 3 high production advisories: `fast-uri` host-confusion ×2 and `find-my-way` HTTP/2 DDoS, all transitive beneath `fastify@5.10.0`, which is already the latest release and pins them itself.

Fixed with **scoped `overrides` in `pnpm-workspace.yaml`** (where this repo keeps pnpm config, alongside `allowBuilds`), not by bumping fastify — these are patch-level bumps, so overrides carry less risk than a framework upgrade. The overrides are **scoped per major** because two `fast-uri` lines are genuinely in use: 3.x under `@fastify/ajv-compiler>ajv`, 4.x under `@fastify/fast-json-stringify`. A blanket override would drag ajv onto 4.x.

**The instructive failure: the first attempt made it worse in a way the audit count hid.** Writing `fast-uri@3: '>=3.1.4'` looks correct but is **unbounded** — `>=3.1.4` admits 4.x, so ajv resolved *up* from 3.1.3 to **4.1.0**, which is itself vulnerable. The advisory count fell 3 → 1, which reads like progress, and the surviving finding was the same package under a different path. Bounding it to `'>=3.1.4 <4'` put ajv on 3.1.4 and closed it. **A semver override with an open upper bound is a version bump in disguise**; when overriding one major of a multi-major dependency, always bound the range.

Verified green after the change: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning), typecheck clean, **399 files / 2821 passed | 1 skipped**, and **e2e 58/58** — run deliberately because `find-my-way` is fastify's actual router, so a routing regression would surface there rather than in unit tests. Diff is `pnpm-lock.yaml` + `pnpm-workspace.yaml` only.

Written from a temporary worktree on `main`; the primary checkout is on another session's `fix/conductor-node-pin-portability` and was not touched.

2026-07-30 **Ledger catch-up — the conductor-portability arc (7/28→7/30), written as one entry because the work landed while STATUS was not being kept.** Board **113 done / 1 blocked** of 114 tickets / 409 pts; the only open item is still W9-08, blocked on a content re-sign rather than on code. Fifteen commits since the last entry, **all confined to `scripts/`** save one dependency fix — `git diff --name-only 369ce85..HEAD` lists `scripts/` and nothing else, which is what licenses the e2e note at the foot of this entry to be a statement about the machine rather than about the product.

**Every one of these was found by pointing the conductor at Kryptkeeper, not by reading it.** That is the arc's single lesson: the harness had been correct for exactly one repo, and each Dokima-shaped assumption in it was invisible to inspection and obvious within seconds of execution.

**W9-12 landed and closed — the wave's last code ticket.** `scripts/models.json` was read at module scope, so a repo importing the harness crashed at IMPORT with a bare `ENOENT scripts/models.json`, before argument parsing, with nothing to indicate a config was missing. Model routing folded into `conductor.config.json`, so the harness needs one config rather than three vendored files plus a config. The follow-up commit (`a45e67f`) guards `conductor.config.json`'s own `JSON.parse` — worth recording that it **relocated the crash rather than removing it**, and was described that way in its own subject line instead of being claimed as a fix.

**Seven portability fixes merged as `fix/conductor-node-pin-portability` (`1ae3324`).** W3-15 makes the Node pin project-configurable (`CONFIG.nvmrcPath`; a bare `readFileSync('.nvmrc')` fataled any repo without a root `.nvmrc`, which is Kryptkeeper — it pins at `ui/.nvmrc`), and takes `supervise.sh`'s branch prefix and worktree dir from config too, so a supervised run in a ported repo cleans up its OWN branches instead of Dokima-named ones. `--no-merge` no longer loops forever re-claiming the same ticket and resetting its branch; the agent's worktree is provisioned and not just the verifier's (without it every ui-lane ticket self-blocked with no `node_modules`); a deliberate self-block is honoured instead of retried; parked and blocked branches are pushed, not just `main`; and `supervise.sh` never deletes a branch carrying unmerged commits.

**Three of those seven exist because the harness destroyed finished work.** S-01 completed, passed gates, reviewed APPROVE, parked on `kk/s-01` — and a re-claim reset the branch ~1ms later, making three commits (637 lines of ops runbooks plus an executed restore drill) unreachable; recovered from dangling objects only. `supervise.sh`'s crash cleanup then deleted a parked branch outright on the next launch. The manual workaround — hand-adding each finished ticket to `holdTickets` and renaming its branch out of the prefix — was performed five times in one session before the fix, and is precisely the bookkeeping a human eventually forgets. **Under `--no-merge`, the branch IS the deliverable**, and every code path that treats a branch as disposable debris has to learn that.

**Four new board lint rules, and the false-positive trap caught three of them on first contact with a real board.** `migrationCollisions` (a runner keying migrations by numeric prefix lets one version's SQL silently overwrite another's) keyed on the version number alone, so two tickets deliberately SHARING a migration file read as a collision — fixed to compare distinct filenames, and recorded plainly that the buggy rule had been merged to main twenty minutes earlier. `pageMountWarning` (a new UI page nothing imports is wired to nothing) produced 12 warnings on its first real run, nearly all false: it fired on tickets EDITING an existing page, which need no route and no nav entry — narrowed to skip `done` tickets and to consider only pages not already tracked by git. `testSiblingWarning` (a ticket whose write_scope can touch implementation but not its test siblings) shipped checking "any test anywhere", so a ticket scoping two impl files plus one unrelated integration test passed clean — fixed to check per implementation file; then 27 of its 29 warnings turned out to be on `done` tickets, whose scope is settled either way, leaving 2 real ones after the guard.

**"A linter that cries wolf gets ignored, which is worse than no linter."** Each of the three was right about the hazard and wrong about which inputs exhibit it — the same error in three different rules, which suggests the check to write next is the one that runs a new rule against the real board before it merges, not after.

**The most expensive single defect was a scope-reachability failure that deleted real tests.** W6-01's agent implemented HA leader election, wrote five table-driven tests plus a prove-the-negative red-check, verified them against a temp SQLite DB — then DELETED them and self-blocked, because `write_scope` listed three `.go` files and no `_test.go` sibling. It obeyed MASTER_PROMPT's "never self-amend write_scope" rule and the harness punished it for doing so. The tests are simply gone. `testSiblingWarning` exists to catch that at filing time; the rule is off by default and config-driven, so the script stays language-agnostic.

**Three more fixes on 7/29–7/30, each a case of the harness charging a ticket for a defect that was never in it.** A `FIX` verdict carrying nothing above MEDIUM produced an EMPTY blocker list, and the loop retried the agent asking it to fix nothing, then blocked the ticket with an empty ledger — "blocked" with no recorded reason, the least useful possible outcome; **the presence of blockers is now the decision, not the verdict string.** A conductor killed mid-ticket left a branch carrying only its claim commit, which `parkedBranchIds()` counted as parked work, so the ticket was skipped forever with nothing to protect and no way back without deleting the branch by hand — a branch whose only commits touch the board is a dead claim. And two unguarded `loadPlan(wt)` calls against a ticket worktree let a vanished worktree's ENOENT kill the entire run, taking unrelated in-flight tickets down as collateral: **a vanished worktree now fails its ticket, not the queue**, with `boardUnreadableGap` kept deliberately distinct from `doneCheckGap` because an unreadable board and a board reporting the wrong status are different failures with different fixes.

**Scope-conditional gates (`{cmd, args, when}`) — and why that is not a relaxation.** A gate whose `when` globs match nothing in the ticket's write_scope is skipped, and the skip is LOGGED (`gates.skip`), since silently running fewer checks than the operator believes is its own hazard. Kryptkeeper's S-32 (write_scope `internal/bootstrap/*` plus `tests/smoke/**`, not one `ui/` path) failed twice on a flaky frontend suite while an unrelated opengrep scan held the machine at load 155 — a gate it could not possibly have affected, charged to the ticket and costing two agent sessions. That repo's own CLAUDE.md already scopes the ui pair to "tickets touching `ui/`"; the harness was over-gating relative to the policy the project states.

**Gate re-verified on this repo today, Node 22.23.1:** `pnpm lint` 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning, an unused eslint-disable in `docs/dogfood/run-dogfood.mjs`), `pnpm typecheck` clean, `pnpm test` **399 files / 2880 passed | 1 skipped**. W9-11's recorded fragility — its one-line-diff test hardcodes ticket W9-09's row from the live `plan.json` — survived the W9-12 filing/claim/close cycle and is green.

**e2e is NOT claimed green, and NOT claimed red.** The run finished **30 failed / 28 passed, in 15.0 minutes** — for a suite that takes ~30s on a quiet machine. (An earlier draft of this entry said "5 of 58" from partial output read while the run was still going; the final count is 30, and the load kept climbing from 100.96 to **154.13** underneath it. Recording the corrected number rather than the first one I saw.) Every failure carries a timeout signature — 23 × `toBeVisible() ... timeout 5000ms`, 25 × `element(s) not found`, 7 × `Test timeout of 30000ms exceeded` — with the Kryptkeeper conductor spawning agent sessions and four concurrent `opengrep-core` processes throughout. This is the same confound `e93a51c` recorded on 7/29, where three runs of unchanged code gave 58 pass, then 1 fail, then 15 fail. The reason to treat it as contention rather than regression is mechanical, not optimistic: **`git diff --name-only 369ce85..HEAD` returns `scripts/` only** — no `apps/`, `packages/`, `content/` or `e2e/` file has changed since the run that last verified 58/58, and `apps/` and `packages/` reference the conductor solely in comments, never by import. **Re-run e2e on a quiet machine before relying on it.** Recording it this way rather than rounding to green is the point; a suite that is red for an environmental reason and reported green is how the 7/23 audit found a plans.spec.ts assertion that had sat silently red since W5-16.

**The unit suite then went red too, at load 154 — and the two failures pin down a real fragility worth naming.** `packages/tickets/src/transitions.property.test.ts` and `packages/harbormaster/test/berths.test.ts` both failed with `Test timed out in 5000ms`, having passed in the 2880/2880 run 25 minutes earlier at load ~100. Both are randomized property tests, and **neither produced an assertion failure or a fast-check counterexample** — fast-check shrinks and prints a violating case when an invariant actually breaks, and it printed none. Running each in isolation still failed (the machine was no quieter), so isolation proved nothing; what discriminated was re-running them **unmodified with `--testTimeout=60000`**, where they pass 1/1 and 12/12 with every assertion green. That is the difference between "the invariant is violated" and "the clock ran out", and only the second is true here.

**No timeout was changed to make this pass, deliberately.** The 5000ms budget is correctly calibrated for a normal machine — these tests have been green across every full gate this project has run, including one earlier today — so widening it would trade a genuine performance canary for a quiet hook. `berths.test.ts` is in any case already recorded in this ledger and in `plan.json` as reproducibly flaky under load, dating to W4-07. The nearest real improvement, if this recurs on a quiet machine, is bounding `fc.assert`'s run count or raising the timeout for these two files specifically with a comment saying why — a ticket, not a drive-by edit made to silence a hook.

**Repo hygiene, same pass.** Deleted **17 fully-merged local branches** (8 `worktree-agent-*` subagent leftovers whose worktrees were already pruned, 3 `sw/*`, 2 `landed/*`, `sec/ledger-atomicity`, `review/design-review-hardening`, 2 `fix/conductor-*`), each verified at zero commits not on `main` before deletion; remote branches deliberately untouched. **`sw/w9-08` kept** — it is the one branch carrying unmerged work, and it is on both remotes. Untracked `docs/work/supervise.pid` (held a dead PID from 7/14; `.gitignore` already covered its `conductor.pid` sibling) and `docs/work/telemetry.jsonl` (appended by `content/validators/_lib.sh` on every validator run and read by nothing — it showed up dirty in ticket after ticket, and the plan notes record at least four separate agents having to reason about whether to stage it). Both files stay on disk; only the tracking stops.

**`docs/ROADMAP.md` was contradicting `plan.json` and is corrected.** It claimed "329 points across 84 tickets" against a real board of **409 across 114**, and five of its nine per-wave rows had drifted (W3 59→62, W4 52→55, **W5 49→78**, W6 28→31, W8 16→29) with the entire W9 wave absent from the table. W9 added with exit criteria written honestly as post-hoc — it was filed after the 1.0 dogfood gate and never had pre-planned ones. The "Resume preconditions" section is marked historical: all three were met, W3 closed long since, and precondition (2) — `supervise.sh`'s hardcoded Node pin — was finally generalized by W3-15 in this very arc.

2026-07-30 (2nd) **W9-13 done — Dokima compiles, and the compiled thing actually runs. Board 114 done / 1 blocked / 1 todo of 116.** `pnpm build` now emits a 428KB plain-JS `apps/server/dist/main.js` that boots under bare `node` with no `tsx` and no TypeScript loader. The packaged install path was not merely missing a build step: `tsx` was an `apps/server` **devDependency** that `cli-entry.mjs` spawned at runtime, and every `@dokima/*` dep was `workspace:*` — a registry consumer would have received neither, so `npx dokima` could never have worked. W8-05 closed naming this an honest gap; it had never been ticketed.

**A probe, run before any code was written, is what made this a real ticket instead of a config change.** esbuild bundles the whole server cleanly — 0 errors, 0 warnings — and the bundle then died on its first execution with `ENOENT ... scandir <bundle-dir>/migrations`. **Ten sites resolved assets by counting `../` hops off `import.meta.url`**, which a bundle invalidates by collapsing ten source directories into one file. The estimate went 5 → 8 points on the strength of that one run. Compiling green and booting are different claims, and only the second one matters.

**The fix is an anchor, not a re-layout.** `distributionRoot()` in `packages/shared/src/paths` walks up for the root `package.json` and matches on its **name**, so `@dokima/*` manifests are walked past — a call site inside `packages/shared` would otherwise stop at its own manifest and silently produce a plausible-looking wrong path, which is the failure mode a test now covers explicitly. `resolveAsset()` then joins the *same repo-relative path the call site already used*, and the tarball preserves that layout (`content/`, `packages/events/migrations`, `apps/web/dist`, `e2e/fitness-fixtures`). One expression is correct from a source checkout and from an installed copy, with no second layout to keep in sync.

**One site was not a path bug and is worth naming.** `onboard-dispatch-port.ts` imported `packages/loop/src/index.ts` — a TypeScript **source** file — by absolute `file://` URL. Its own header admitted this was a write_scope workaround rather than a design choice. It fails packaging twice over: the hops break, and plain `node` cannot import `.ts` at all once `tsx` is gone. Since this ticket's scope included `apps/server/package.json`, `@dokima/loop` became a declared dependency and the specifier a bare one — still a dynamic `import()`, which preserves both the lazy load and the `loadLoopModuleForTests` seam, but now statically analysable. **A workaround that outlives the constraint that caused it becomes an invisible defect**; the only reason this one was findable is that the header said why it existed.

**Proven by execution, not by exit 0.** `pnpm pack` → extract → run the `bin` under plain `node`, in a tree with no `tsx` and no `.ts` sources and no `workspace:*` in the manifest: `/healthz` → `{"status":"ok","db":true,"ws":true}` (**`db:true` is the load-bearing assertion** — it is precisely what the ENOENT killed), SPA shell 200, `/api/v1/roster` 200, `/api/v1/guide/first-run-wizard` 200 / 1471 bytes. Five deps stay external (`better-sqlite3` is a native addon; the rest are ordinary runtime deps) and are now declared on the root package — they had been living in **three different packages'** `node_modules`, which is why a naive ship would have died at `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`. Gate: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning), typecheck clean, **400 files / 2885 passed | 1 skipped**.

**And the machine finally went quiet, which settled the e2e question this ledger has been carrying all day.** Same suite, same code path: **30 failed at load 154, 2 failed at load 10**, and the runtime fell from 15.0 minutes to 3.7. The two survivors are `trace.spec.ts:54` and `:73`, and they are **not** a W9-13 regression — established rather than asserted, by a byte-identical failure signature in this session's *first* e2e run against unmodified `main`: same locator, same `openFreshProject` helper line, differing only in the random project UUID. Filed as **W9-14** rather than folded into this ticket, so a packaging commit does not quietly absorb an unrelated UI defect. The suspected cause is already in the log — `computeProjectStats open failed ... Cannot open database because the directory does not exist` — and W9-14 says plainly that widening the assertion's timeout is not an acceptable fix if the card genuinely never renders.

**Process note against myself.** The first filing of W9-13 was rejected by `validate-plan.mjs` twice — 8 acceptance criteria against a cap of 6, then a missing `stories` key — and its `write_scope` (tsconfigs + package.jsons) could not have held the fix that was ultimately needed. Both were caught before an agent was dispatched rather than after one walked into the wall, which is the W9-06/W9-08 lesson working as intended, but it is worth recording that the scope was wrong on the first pass *even with that lesson explicitly in mind*.

2026-07-30 (3rd) **W9-14 done — the e2e suite was never hermetic, and the "flaky" trace specs were reporting a real 10x slowdown. Board 115 done / 1 blocked / 1 todo of 117.** `playwright.config.ts` points `DOKIMA_HOME` at a **fixed** tmp path shared by every test *and every run*, with `reuseExistingServer` locally, so nothing ever cleaned the Fleet registry it holds. It had reached **1,164 projects / 422KB of `fleet.json`**, accumulating since 2026-07-23 — every project every test had ever onboarded, almost all pointing at tmp directories long since deleted.

**The ticket's own leading hypothesis was wrong, which is worth recording.** W9-14 was filed pointing at `computeProjectStats open failed ... Cannot open database because the directory does not exist` as the likely cause. That line appears on a **fully green run too** — tests legitimately delete their own tmp dirs — so it was a red herring, and following it would have led into the stats code, which is not where the defect lives.

**What actually discriminated was a wipe, not a theory.** Clearing that home and re-running the identical suite: **58/58 in 22.0s**, against 2 failed in 3.7m minutes earlier. Same code, same machine, same specs. Two other candidates were killed with measurements rather than argument first: `GET /api/v1/projects` answered in **286ms** with all 1,164 loaded (so the server was never the bottleneck, and the cost is client-side render volume), and `FleetHome` renders `sorted.map(...)` with no cap or pagination, so the card was genuinely absent from the DOM rather than merely scrolled away.

**`trace.spec.ts` was not touched, deliberately.** Its assertion was correct the whole time — a newly-onboarded project's card really did not appear within 5s — and widening that timeout would have converted a truthful failure into silent tolerance of a suite that had gone from 22 seconds to 3.7 minutes. This is the same principle as the 2026-07-30 unit-suite entry: the fix for a red test is not always in the test.

**Fixed in the harness, where the defect actually was:** a Playwright `globalSetup` clears `DOKIMA_HOME`, the state DB, and its `-wal`/`-shm` siblings before every run, with `e2e/env-paths.ts` shared by the config and the setup so the two cannot drift onto different directories — a stale copy in one would silently clear a directory nobody uses while the real one kept growing.

**Verified the hard way, per the ticket's own acceptance.** A fix that only works from an already-clean home has not fixed anything, so the genuine 1,164-project `fleet.json` was restored and the suite run against it: **58/58 green**, `globalSetup` having cleared it. Then three consecutive full runs, 58/58 each, with the registry holding steady at **33 entries — one run's worth — instead of climbing 1164 → 1197 → 1230**. Wall clock converged 1.8m → 56s → 21.9s. Gate: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning), typecheck clean, **400 files / 2885 passed | 1 skipped**.

**The product question it exposed is filed as W9-15, not smuggled in here.** There is no remove/forget path for a project anywhere — no DELETE route in `apps/server/src/api/projects.ts`, no affordance in `apps/web/src/fleet/**` — so an entry whose directory is deleted is permanent, and renders as an ordinary card with silently zeroed stats, indistinguishable from a real empty project. "Prune entries whose directory is missing" would have been a product behaviour change disguised as a test fix. W9-15 also names the sharp edge explicitly: removing a project from the Fleet must never delete the user's repo or its `state.db`.

**Third scope miss of the session, and the pattern is now clear enough to name.** W9-14's `write_scope` was `trace.spec.ts` + `src/**` + the server API — and the fix lives in `playwright.config.ts` and `e2e/**`, neither of which was in it. Same shape as W9-06 (unreachable imports) and W9-13 (tsconfigs that could not hold a bundler). In all three the scope was written from **where the symptom appeared**, not from where the cause turned out to live — and the symptom's location is precisely what a pre-dispatch reachability check cannot know. That is an argument for scoping a diagnosis ticket loosely and tightening it once root-caused, rather than for checking harder up front.

2026-07-30 (4th) **W9-16 done — one-command autorun, and the supervisor no longer destroys uncommitted work on launch. Board 116 done / 1 blocked / 1 todo of 118.** `pnpm autorun` (or `scripts/autorun.sh start|stop|status`) wraps what CONDUCTOR_RUNBOOK.md had you hand-type: `nohup` + `caffeinate` + the supervisor + the log redirect + the pid write. Extra arguments pass straight through, so `--waves`, `--no-merge` and `--breakpoint` are unchanged. `--escalate` stays opt-in, per `conductor.config.json`'s `$modelsNote` (D-018 — frontier spend requires a human typing it), because a convenience wrapper that quietly raises a spend ceiling is not a convenience.

**The wrapper is the small half. The defect it uncovered is the real content of this ticket.** `supervise.sh` runs `git checkout -f main` and `git clean -fd` at the **top of its launch loop** — every start, not merely after a crash, despite the comment describing it as crash recovery. Launching from a feature branch with work in progress therefore destroys that work silently.

**Found by losing this ticket's own first draft to it.** The first `autorun.sh start --lint` reset the tree out from under the session: the uncommitted W9-16 board filing was discarded and the untracked `scripts/autorun.sh` was deleted outright, seconds after being written. The evidence is unusually direct — the tool being built ate itself on its first run.

**Same shape as the 2026-07-28 fix, one layer down.** That one stopped this cleanup deleting unmerged *branches*, after it destroyed a parked, review-APPROVED Kryptkeeper branch. It protected committed work on branches and left **uncommitted and untracked** work exposed. The generalization neither fix made until now: *this cleanup cannot distinguish debris from work in progress, so the distinction has to come from when it runs.* A first launch is a deliberate human act; iteration 2+ genuinely is a dead attempt's leftovers. So the guard is time-based, not content-based — refuse on the first iteration, keep resetting on crash-restarts exactly as before.

**Guarded in both layers, deliberately not one.** `autorun.sh` checks before anything is backgrounded, so the refusal lands on your terminal rather than in a log file you have to know to go read; `supervise.sh` checks independently, because ported repos vendor *that* file and would otherwise inherit the footgun with no wrapper in sight. Both name the branch and list what they would have destroyed.

**Verified without spending a provider token.** Launch via `--lint` (the conductor exits immediately): pid file written, log appended, process reaped, `status` honest before and after, board untouched. Then the destructive case directly — an untracked `CANARY.txt` **survived** both an `autorun.sh start` and a bare `bash scripts/supervise.sh`, each of which would previously have deleted it. Gate: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning).

**Worth stating plainly for whoever reads this cold:** Dokima is where `conductor.mjs` and `supervise.sh` were built, and from which Kryptkeeper vendored them. The harness was always here — only the front door was missing, and the other repo effectively had one because its invocation was typed once and left running. The cost of that asymmetry was a launch path nobody exercised often enough to notice it was destructive.

2026-08-02 W9-15 done — Fleet can forget a project, and a vanished directory says so. `apps/web/src/fleet/**`, `apps/server/src/api/projects.ts`.
