The ticket asked for a decision before building; both halves were needed and both shipped. (a) `ProjectCard.available` is a filesystem fact computed per read — when the directory is gone `toCard` doesn't even probe the DB, since the stats would be zeros either way and zeros alone are indistinguishable from a real empty project (the honest-absence rule `healthz` and the trace view already follow). The card renders a distinct dashed **Unavailable** state naming the missing path, with no Open/Archive and no counters at all rather than zeroed ones. (b) `removeProject` + `DELETE /api/v1/projects/:id` (204) forget the registry entry. **The sharp edge is structural, not merely tested:** `removeProject` has no filesystem write path other than `saveRegistry`, so it cannot delete a repo or its `.dokima/state.db`.

**Three mutations, three reds — the gates are real, not read.** (1) `available = true` → the vanished-directory test reds alone, 581 others stay green. (2) `removeProject` also `fs.rm`s the project dir → both the unit sharp-edge test AND the route test red, each asserting against the filesystem rather than the API response. (3) `if (!card.available)` → `if (false)` in the card → the e2e reds on the missing `data-unavailable` attribute.

**The feature exposed a latent test defect.** `app.spec.ts`'s `getByText('Dokima')` matched case-insensitively and substring-wise, so once an unavailable card began rendering its `/tmp/dokima-*` path it hit 4 elements and violated strict mode. That assertion was always one stale registry entry from failing; it is now scoped to `.app-shell__header`. Also worth noting: `pnpm test` stayed green through a real type error — two `ProjectCard` fixtures were missing the new required `available` field, and only `tsc` caught it. Law 3 runs both for exactly this reason.

**SCOPE NOTE (schema defect, not a workaround).** W9-15's declared `write_scope` is `apps/web/src/fleet/**`, `apps/server/src/api/projects.ts`, `apps/server/src/api/server/**` — which does not cover the tests its own acceptance criteria mandate. Writing them required `apps/server/src/api/projects.test.ts`, `apps/web/e2e/fleet.spec.ts`, `apps/web/e2e/app.spec.ts` and `apps/web/src/onboarding/FirstRunWizard.test.tsx` (the last two forced by the change, not chosen). This is precisely the upstream class captured as W10-07 in `docs/work/W10_PLAN.md` — attest v3.1.9–11's `TEST_SIBLING_STRICT`, "a write_scope must cover its own tests". Recorded rather than silently widened.

Gate: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning), typecheck clean, **400 files / 2888 passed | 3 skipped** (+5), **59 e2e passed** (+1).

2026-08-02 W9-08 done — the phase gate can pass again. Blocked since 2026-07-28, and **the block was never about the code**: the fix had been complete and green on `sw/w9-08` for five days, unable to merge because editing a byte of `content/validators/validate-mermaid.sh` invalidates its hash in the signed pack, and re-signing needs a private key nobody had. The 2026-08-02 key rotation (forced by the leaked-key incident) produced that key as a side effect, so this ticket unblocked itself. Cherry-picked `4df70ad` onto the post-rename main — clean, all four in-scope files, no stale `SHIPWRIGHT_*` in the picked content — then re-signed.

**Two defects, both reporting bugs, neither touching a validator's judgement (AC5).** Defect 1: `validate-mermaid.sh` was the only validator that never sourced `_lib.sh`, so a clean scan exited 0 with zero-byte stdout; `parseValidatorOutput('')` correctly returns null and `run.ts` normalizes that to `exitCode: 2` — so the validator every phase requires could never pass. Fixed at the script, not by loosening the contract, exactly as AC1 preferred: treating empty stdout as "clean" would have weakened the fail-closed guarantee for *every* validator. Defect 2: `parseValidatorOutput` ran `JSON.parse` on the whole trimmed stdout first, and a single NDJSON finding line **is** valid JSON — so it parsed, fell past the array/envelope branches, and hit `return null`, reporting one real gap as malformed. Two-plus lines only ever worked by accident (concatenated objects aren't valid JSON, throwing into the NDJSON fallback). Line count no longer changes what counts as a finding.

**The block, reproduced before fixing it.** `doctor` on the cherry-picked tree: `[FAIL] pack-signatures: 1 file(s) failed hash verification`. After `sign-content.mjs`: `[OK] pack-signatures: manifest + all file hashes verified`. That is the whole ticket in two lines.

**Mutations — and one that had to be corrected.** (A) Silencing the envelope again (`validator_exit() { exit 0; }`) reds both `run.test.ts` proofs: clean run → exitCode 0, and single real finding → exitCode 1 not 2. (B) The first attempt at Defect 2's mutation replaced the `return parseNdjson(trimmed)` inside the **catch block** — the pre-existing multi-line path — and redded the *two-line* test while the dedicated single-line fixture stayed green. That is a mutation aimed at the wrong subject, and it would have "verified" the wrong code. Retargeted at the **fallthrough** return (the actual Defect-2 fix), it reds the two tests that matter: `parses a single NDJSON finding line` and `treats a single non-envelope JSON object as one NDJSON finding`. Worth recording: breaking the subject only proves something when you break the *right* subject.

**AC4 holds** — `exitCode: 2` stays "verdict untrustworthy" and was not widened: empty stdout, non-JSON garbage, partially-parsing NDJSON, timeout and spawn error each still yield 2, each pinned by a test, all green.

Gate: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning), typecheck clean, **400 files / 2891 passed | 3 skipped** (+3), **59 e2e passed**. `doctor: OK`.

**Board: 118/118. Every ticket W0-01…W9-16 is `done`.**

2026-08-02 W10-01 done — the provider registry exists, and it is the same key the CLI already read. `packages/gateway/src/registry/**`, `apps/server/src/api/server/providers-{store,routes}.ts`. `cli/ops/providers-core.ts` said it verbatim — "There is no persisted provider registry yet anywhere in the codebase" — and read a bare `providers` settings key consumed only by `doctor` and `providers refresh`: no REST, no GUI, so registering a provider meant hand-editing JSON.

**The design decision worth recording: it stays that same settings key.** A new table would have needed a migration (`packages/events/migrations/**`, outside scope) and — far worse — created a second source of truth where the GUI and `doctor` disagree about which providers exist. Reusing the key inherits three guarantees rather than reimplementing them: run>project>global precedence (FR-S1), a `settings.changed` event per write (FR-S3), and the file layer's secret refusal (FR-S2). The round-trip test asserts the bytes land under `providers` in `.dokima/settings.json` — i.e. exactly where `getEffectiveSettings` looks.

**Credential refusal is at the boundary, not the store.** A literal key is rejected *wholesale before validation or persistence* — "stored then scrubbed" would both write it to disk and leave the caller believing it was accepted. Proved against the bytes on disk, not the API response.

**Two mutations, both red.** (1) Disabling the secret scan reds the literal-credential test alone. (2) Disabling the D-019 consent check reds both the unit and route copilot tests — Copilot cannot be enabled through the registry without a ledgered ack, and a *disabled* copilot entry is still allowed, so the gate is a gate rather than a wall.

**Prerequisite discharged (the one the plan flagged):** `packages/gateway/package.json` published only `./src/index.ts`, and that export map is the *stated* reason `harbormaster/src/loop-land-policy.ts` reimplements escalation policy locally instead of importing it. Now publishes `./registry`, `./routing` and `./escalation` as well, so the seam does not get reimplemented a fourth time.

**SCOPE NOTE.** Registering the routes required one import + one call in `settings-routes.ts`, outside the declared write_scope — the build-then-wire seam again (routes nobody can reach are a disconnected pipeline, not a deliverable). Same class as W9-15's note and W10-07's `TEST_SIBLING_STRICT`. Recorded, not silently widened.

Gate: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning), typecheck clean, **402 files / 2905 passed | 3 skipped** (+14), **59 e2e passed**, 7/7 boundary red fixtures proven failing-capable.

2026-08-02 W10-03 done — the model the user picks is now the model that runs. `apps/server/src/api/pipeline/model-resolution.ts` (new), `gateway-model-port.ts`. The defect, stated exactly as it was: the ONLY production model-call path in the product built a hardcoded `createOaiCompatProvider` from three environment variables, and neither call site imported the router, `resolveModelChain`, the matrix or the registry. So five finished, contract-tested adapters (W2-01…09) were never constructed on any production path — only by their own tests — and the matrix was read by `roster-resolve.ts` for DISPLAY only, meaning the UI could advertise a model the pipeline would never call.

**Resolution order is explicit and both directions are pinned:** an explicit registry+matrix selection wins; the env vars survive as the documented CI/fixture override (the e2e fake-model gateway depends on them) and deliberately LOSE to a real selection. With nothing configured, env still wins — a first run must keep working offline (C-1), not hard-fail.

**Model→provider binding uses the `<providerId>/<model>` prefix** already half-present in the codebase (`matrix-routes.ts` flags Copilot rows by a `copilot/` prefix). An unprefixed model binds when exactly one provider is enabled and is otherwise reported as ambiguous — never guessed. A disabled provider is refused rather than silently used.

**`route()` deliberately, not a bare `resolveModelChain`:** that keeps maker≠verifier STRUCTURAL (C-4/Law 5), so wiring the registry in cannot become a way around the guard. A verifier landing on the maker's model still throws `SameModelRefusedError` through the new path — asserted, not assumed.

**THE MUTATION IS THE ACCEPTANCE.** Reverting only the resolution call site to env-only — the exact pre-ticket behaviour — reds **8 of 11** seam tests, including "routes to the provider the MATRIX names, not the env default". That is the W9-11 test applied to the bug it would have caught, and it was executed, not asserted.

**One thing refused rather than faked.** `providerForConfig` now dispatches on kind, which makes ollama/lm-studio reachable from production for the first time. The cloud kinds (anthropic/openai/vertex/copilot) throw a NAMED refusal instead: `AnthropicConfig.costTable` is required with no $0 default ("no $0 default for a paid API" — its own header), and the adapter needs a resolved secret rather than the `credentialRef` the registry stores. Fabricating either would have been a lie about cost or a credential leak. A user selecting a cloud provider gets an explanation, never a silent fallback to localhost. **HANDOFF:** keychain resolution + a real price table is the follow-up that makes cloud kinds constructible.

Gate: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning), typecheck clean, **403 files / 2918 passed | 3 skipped** (+13), **59 e2e passed**.

2026-08-02 W10-06 done — a real token scale, no raw hexes, and a board that fits its pane. `apps/web/src/styles.css`, `styles.test.ts`, 14 feature stylesheets, `board/board.css`. Five tokens became 38; 54 hex literals outside `styles.css` became zero.

**The defect was worse than "hardcoded colours", and the survey is what found it.** Eight tokens — `--sw-success`, `--sw-warning`, `--sw-attention`, `--sw-muted`, `--sw-fg-muted`, `--color-text-muted`, `--color-border`, `--color-warning` — were referenced through `var(--x, #literal)` and **defined nowhere**. Every one of those fallbacks always won, so roughly a third of the "tokenised" declarations were rendering a frozen literal while reading as theme-aware. The same role was improvised per file: six different reds, five ambers, five greens.

**Six live WCAG 2.2 AA failures fell out of that, and the migration fixes them.** Muted text had no dark value at all — `#666` on the dark background is **3.20:1**. The active Settings tab put `#fff` on `var(--sw-accent)`, which flips to a *light* blue in dark theme: **2.58:1**. Three improvised warning/attention colours were below 4.5:1 in light theme (`#e0a800` at **2.15:1**, `#b8860b` at 3.25:1, `#d64545` at 4.38:1), and the notification badge's white-on-`#d64545` was 4.38:1. Every replacement value was measured against the background it actually sits on, in both themes, before being committed — the ratios are in `styles.css` beside each token. Axe never caught these because the affected states (stale/waived badges) aren't in the seeded fixtures.

**The gate is the deliverable, and it was written before the migration** so it was red against all 54 violations on its first run rather than green-by-construction afterwards. Hex is legal only on a `--sw-*` definition line in `styles.css` — no allowlist, nothing to argue about. Raw px gets three exempt properties (`box-shadow`, `background-size`, `background-position`), each with a written reason, because a px there is shadow/gradient geometry and not a spacing step. A fourth assertion bans `var()` fallbacks outright, since that is precisely where the eight phantom tokens hid. **Three planted defects verified red:** a raw hex, a raw px, and a re-introduced phantom fallback. A fifth test guards the guard — a bad glob would make the whole thing vacuously pass, which is how a lint rule dies quietly.

**The board fits now, and the fix is grid rather than `flex-wrap` for a measured reason.** Six 12rem columns needed ~1200px in a pane that is **471px** at 1440x900; the shipped tour capture shows "In Pro…" clipped with columns 4-6 off-pane entirely, and *both* board e2e specs force a 2400px viewport to work around it. `flex-wrap` was tried first and rejected on the screenshot: `flex-grow` stretches whatever lands on the last row, so a 4+2 wrap rendered Blocked and Done at double width and read as a rendering bug. `auto-fit` + `minmax(--sw-board-column-min, 1fr)` gives equal columns and aligned rows. 9rem yields 2 per row at 219px; 8.5rem fits 3 at 143px but wrapped "Move ticket E2E-1" mid-identifier — the same defect class the ticket exists to remove — so 9rem it is.

**The W9-05 edge fade was checked for deadness, not assumed alive.** Leaving its declarations in place purely to green `Lane.test.tsx` (which reads raw CSS text and is outside this ticket's write_scope) would have been exactly the kind of fossil Law 4 exists to prevent. Measured instead: `.split-pane__pane` is draggable well below one column's minimum, and at a 120px pane the strip scrolls 128px of content against a 95px viewport. The affordance still has a width range where it does its job.

**PROCESS TRAP, recorded because it cost a gate cycle.** Claiming the ticket with `JSON.stringify(plan, null, 2)` rewrote **460 lines** of `plan.json`: the file is stored ASCII-escaped (`§`, `—`) and naive re-serialisation emits literal UTF-8. W9-11's byte-preservation test caught it — "expected 0 to be greater than 2000" — but only in the full suite. Use `writePlan()` from `scripts/conductor-lib.mjs`, never a bare stringify. Trap #4 also landed as advertised: `pnpm test` was green while `tsc` had three errors in the new test file.

**APPEARANCE CHANGED, not just contrast — say so before someone diffs screenshots.** Consolidating six reds, five ambers and five greens onto three roles visibly restyles things that were never contrast failures: the Fleet heartbeat dot goes from `#2ecc71` neon to `#1a7f37` forest in light theme, its stale variant from `#e67e22` orange to `#b45309` amber, and the roster fitness-badge borders from `#3a3`/`#a33`/`#aa3` to the semantic set. **Both themes were rendered and looked at, not just measured** — Fleet, Board, Settings and Roster in dark, plus the Fleet card in light. The two fixes are visible: dark-theme stat labels went from barely-there grey to legible, and the active Settings tab is now dark-on-blue instead of white-on-light-blue.

**The tour captures under `docs/tour/img/` are now stale in two ways** and should not be read as current: they predate the Dokima rename (the header still says "Shipwright") and `05-board-seeded.png` shows a six-across board layout the code no longer produces. `board.css` cites that image as the evidence for the clipping defect, which is correct as history. `docs/tour/` is outside this ticket's write_scope, so regenerating it needs its own ticket.

**HONEST SCOPE NOTE.** This is the token/layout pass only. 112 spacing declarations still sit off the 0.25rem grid and four font sizes sit off the type scale — snapping those changes layout, which is a design decision rather than a script, so only exact on-scale values were migrated (zero visual drift, asserted by the migration script itself). That work plus the typography-hierarchy defects the tour exposes is **filed as W10-28**, as this ticket's acceptance required rather than smuggled in here.

Gate: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning), typecheck clean, **403 files / 2923 passed | 3 skipped** (+5), **59 e2e passed**, 3/3 planted design-gate defects proven failing-capable.

2026-08-02 W10-05 done — UX_SPEC gains the provider/model surface it never had. `docs/design/UX_SPEC.md` 209 → 318 lines; new §6a plus two rows in §2b's empty-state table. §6 had promised that "provider onboarding … lives here (D-007)" and then never said what *here* looked like, so W10-04 would have been invented at the keyboard.

**The spec is written against shapes that already exist, not against an imagined API.** Every field, kind and refusal binds to `packages/gateway/src/registry/` (`ProviderKind`, `ProviderEntry`, the `rule` string on `ProviderRegistryError`) and the three live routes. The field set is specified as a *function of the kind*, which is what the code already enforces: only `oai-compat` requires a `baseUrl` (`ENDPOINT_KINDS`), ollama/lm-studio prefill an editable localhost default, and the fixed-endpoint cloud kinds show no endpoint field at all.

**Quoted copy is verbatim, and that is checked rather than intended.** The cloud-kind refusal and the maker≠verifier refusal appear in the spec as the exact strings `gateway-model-port.ts` and `routing/maker-verifier.ts` raise, and the cited rule names (`missing-base-url`, `invalid-base-url-scheme`, `consent-required`, …) all exist in `registry/validate.ts`. A refusal the user reads should be the refusal the code raised; a spec that paraphrases its own error messages licenses implementation to soften them.

**Eight states are written out, and the distinctions between them are the point.** Unreachable, reachable-but-serves-nothing, and offline-so-the-bundled-catalog-answers are three different things that all look like "no models" if you let them collapse — the honest-absence rule (W9-15) applied to discovery. A selected model that vanishes from an endpoint is marked missing rather than silently rewritten or dropped, and a disabled provider stays visible and greyed because a refusal to *use* is not a reason to *hide*.

**Two destructive affordances have their copy pinned so implementation cannot soften them.** The Copilot consent (D-019) names the undocumented `copilot_internal` API and says plainly that enforcement can permanently ban the user's GitHub account, that the risk is theirs and not Dokima's, and that enabling is ledgered; declining leaves the provider registered and disabled, never half-enabled. Removing a provider states what it does NOT touch — the keychain entry survives, the endpoint's own models survive, and prior receipts survive because the log is append-only (C-6).

2026-08-02 W10-02 done — model catalog + discovery: `listModels()` already existed and `dokima providers refresh` already called it, then discarded the result after computing `modelCount`. New `packages/gateway/src/catalog/` module (`resolveProviderCatalog`) persists and exposes it, backed by a bundled offline catalog (`content/model-catalog/catalog.v1.json`, versioned + provenance-headed like the rest of `content/`) for zero-network first run (Law 9 / C-1).

**"Persist" was implemented as carry-through-and-expose, not durable storage.** The write_scope (widened onto its own acceptance-named files by the prior claim commit) has no store or API route in it — `GET /models` from `docs/API_DESIGN.md` and the registry-backed persistence are later tickets' job (W10-04's Providers panel is the documented consumer). This ticket's surface is the reusable resolver both of those build on: `resolveProviderCatalog()` returns which source answered (`'discovered'` vs `'bundled'`) and, when unreachable, the failure reason — never a silently empty list, never a fabricated one (W9-15's honest-absence rule applied to discovery, per `docs/design/UX_SPEC.md` §6a). `dokima providers refresh` now prints the actual model ids instead of just a count, and distinguishes reachable-but-empty from unreachable from offline-bundled-fallback — three states that all look like "no models" if collapsed.

**`oai-compat` deliberately has no bundled entry.** Ollama (a real public library) and LM Studio (matched to the ids already recorded in `packages/gateway/src/providers/fixtures.ts`) get bundled reference lists; an arbitrary user-supplied OpenAI-compatible endpoint has no knowable catalog, so unreachable + `source: null` + reason is the honest answer, not an oversight.

**Bundled catalog provenance is stated, not implied.** The JSON file's own `provenance` field says the entries are curated by the implementing agent from training-data knowledge of each ecosystem as of 2026-08-02, not fetched from any live registry — `models.dev`-style remote discovery was evaluated and explicitly not built (acceptance criterion: "a remote registry may be optional; it may never be required"). Contract-tested against the recorded LM Studio/Ollama `/v1/models` fixture via the real `OaiCompatProvider` factories (`createLmStudioProvider`/`createOllamaProvider`), never a live call.

2026-08-02 W10-02 retry — external verification of the first "done" pass found `resolveProviderCatalog`'s catch block called `loadBundled(kind)` unguarded: a bundled-catalog load failure (malformed/missing `content/model-catalog/catalog.v1.json`, or a raw ENOENT) would reject the promise instead of resolving, contradicting `resolve.ts`'s own "never silently empty" contract and crashing every provider in `dokima providers refresh`'s `Promise.all` rather than reporting just the affected one unreachable. Fixed by wrapping the `loadBundled` call in its own try/catch inside `resolveProviderCatalog`'s existing catch block: a bundled-load failure now degrades to `{status: 'unreachable', source: null, models: [], reason: <discovery error> + <bundled-load error>}` instead of rejecting. New RED FIXTURE test injects a throwing loader and asserts the promise still resolves with that shape. `resolveAsset()`'s own possible failure to locate the distribution root happens at module-import time (`MODEL_CATALOG_CONTENT_PATH` is a top-level const, same pattern as `fitness/fixtures.ts`'s `FIXTURES_DIR`) and is a pre-existing cross-cutting risk outside this ticket's `write_scope`, not something `resolveProviderCatalog` can catch after the fact.

''}${urlPath}`, but every `urlPath` in that module already hardcodes `/api/v1` — App.tsx passes `baseUrl="/api/v1"` into `EvidencePanel`/`CommandPalette` (correct for `board/api.ts`, whose urlPaths carry no prefix and rely on baseUrl for it), so those two consumers doubled it while `ArtifactViewerRoot.tsx` (no baseUrl passed, outside this ticket's `write_scope`) stayed correct by accident. 2026-08-02 W10-30 done — anchors had no colour rule anywhere in `apps/web/src/**/*.css`, so every `<a>` fell back to the UA default `#0000EE`: 1.95:1 against `--sw-bg` in the dark theme (needs 4.5:1), 8.59:1 in light. Author styles beat the UA stylesheet regardless of specificity, so this also overrides the default `:visited` purple with no extra rule.

2026-08-02 W10-32 done — `.sr-only` was applied at five call sites (`board/Card.tsx`, `drawer/DagEditPanel.tsx`, `SpendByRung.tsx`, `ContractPanel.tsx`, `palette/CommandPalette.tsx`) and defined in zero of the app's fifteen stylesheets, so every board card printed "Move ticket E2E-1" as visible product copy above a `<select>` already labelled "Move to…" — screen-reader users heard the label twice, sighted users saw a bug on the signature surface, in both themes, on all six seeded board captures. Fixed once, globally: `.sr-only` in `styles.css` using the standard visually-hidden technique (`clip-path: inset(50%)` plus a legacy `clip: rect(0 0 0 0)`, `position: absolute` with a non-zero box so it stays in the accessibility tree — never `display: none`, which would also break the existing `getByLabel('Move ticket E2E-DRAG-1')` a11y e2e assertion that already depended on this text being programmatically associated). Sized off `--sw-space-0-5` rather than a bare `1px` so the rule doesn't reintroduce the raw-px pattern the W10-06 gate bans outside the token block.

**The causal link to the board column floor, confirmed by re-measurement, not assumed.** `board.css` recorded that 8.5rem "wrapped 'Move ticket E2E-1' mid-identifier," which is why W10-06 raised `--sw-board-column-min` to 9rem, dropping the six lifecycle states from 3 columns per row to 2. That measurement was taken against text that was never meant to paint. With `.sr-only` now defined, re-verified directly against the real server + real event log (six tickets seeded, one per lifecycle column) at 1440x900 and 1280x800, both themes, `--sw-board-column-min` back at 8.5rem: all six columns render unclipped, no wrapped identifiers, zero visible "Move ticket" text in the DOM. 1440 goes 3-per-row (each track 143px, matching the comment's own "8.5rem fits 3 columns per row at 143px each"); 1280 stays 2-per-row — arithmetic, not a judgment call: the board pane is ~393px wide at 1280, and three 8.5rem tracks need 3×136 + 2×8(gap) = 424px, which doesn't fit at either 8.5rem or the old 9rem (3×144+16 = 448px) — 1280 was 2-per-row before this ticket and is 2-per-row after; only 1440 (2→3) changes. Floor dropped back to 8.5rem; the pipeline reads as six states again at the width where it always could.

**RED FIXTURE, per acceptance**: same blind spot as W10-30/W10-31, one level up — those guard a missing *property* on a known selector, this guards a missing *selector* entirely. `styles.test.ts` scans every `.tsx` for a literal `className="sr-only"` (proving the check isn't vacuous) and every stylesheet for a `.sr-only` rule; reverting just the new CSS rule reds the "is defined in at least one stylesheet" assertion — verified directly: deleting the `.sr-only` rule from `styles.css` while leaving its own explanatory comment (and board.css's, both of which write the literal string `.sr-only` in prose) intact reds exactly that one assertion, 29 passed. Scoped to an explicit `UTILITY_CLASSES` list (currently just `sr-only`), not a fully generic className sweep — the codebase has ~60 other classes applied with no matching CSS rule that are legitimate unstyled semantic hooks (e.g. `board-card__type`), and flagging those would be false positives outside this ticket's `write_scope`. Five gate-integrity tests (docs/TESTING.md §6) feed synthetic component/stylesheet pairs through the same helpers: catches the real-world shape, doesn't false-positive on a longer class sharing the prefix (`.sr-only-legacy`) or on the class name appearing only in a comment, passes once defined, and doesn't flag a class no component applies.

**Gate-integrity fix (first attempt's escaped bug, caught by external review before this pass)**: the first pass's `classIsDefined()` regex-matched `.sr-only` against the raw stylesheet text, comments included — and this ticket's own explanatory comments (`styles.css`, `board.css`) write the literal string `.sr-only` in prose right next to the real rule. Verified empirically before fixing: deleting the real rule while leaving those comments in place left all 29 tests green, because the prose satisfied the same regex the gate used to detect the rule — the exact false-pass a reviewer would need to demonstrate to fail this ticket, and it worked. Fixed by stripping `/* ... */` comments before matching and requiring the match be followed by an actual rule opener (`[^{}]*\{`), plus a new red-fixture test that reproduces the exact comment-adjacent-to-rule shape and asserts it still reads as undefined once the rule itself is gone. Re-ran the same delete-the-rule experiment after the fix: the suite now reds on the intended assertion instead of passing (see RED FIXTURE paragraph above).

Board layout re-verified directly (real server, real event log, all six lifecycle states seeded): 3-per-row at 1440x900, 2-per-row (unclipped) at 1280x800, both themes, zero visible "Move ticket" text in the DOM.

Wrapped the panel in a new `.shortcuts-overlay__backdrop` — fixed, full-viewport `rgb(0 0 0 / 45%)` scrim, matching `command-palette__backdrop`'s existing pattern exactly — that flex-centers the panel with `padding-top: 9rem`. Fixed rem, not `vh`: the header stack is a content-driven height (app-shell header + Fleet's own toolbar), not a viewport-proportional one, so a `vh` offset that clears it at 1440x900 can clip it again at 1280x800. No focus trap added — kept `role="dialog"` with no `aria-modal` and no forced focus move, since neither is paired with the other here (unlike `TicketDrawer`/`CommandPalette`, which both move focus into the panel on open): `aria-modal` alone would hide the rest of the page from assistive tech while focus stayed parked on a now-hidden element, which is worse than today, not better. Escape-to-close (`isShortcutOverlayCloseKey`, pre-existing) is untouched and the `app.spec.ts` e2e assertion for it still passes unmodified.

`shortcuts.ts:14-17`'s static two-row list (`?`, `Esc`) never mentioned ⌘K/Ctrl+K even though `CommandPalette` (`palette/shortcuts.ts`, W4-12) is real and wired inside every open project, and said nothing when Cmd+K is inert on the Fleet screen either. The static `SHORTCUTS` export is now `shortcutsFor({ paletteActive })`, a pure function that appends the palette row only when the palette is actually mounted. `ShortcutsOverlay.tsx` computes `paletteActive` from the URL directly — `?project=` present AND no `?view=` layered over it, mirroring `App.tsx`'s own `CommandPalette` mount condition (verified against `App.tsx:290-320`'s view ternary: Roster/Settings/Plan/Trace/Wizard all replace the project canvas and unmount the palette with it) — rather than a prop threaded down, since the overlay mounts outside `App.tsx`'s project/view ternary by design and `App.tsx` sits outside this ticket's `write_scope`.

`styles.css:200-204`'s `<dt>` had no width, so each row's key/description boundary depended on that row's own `<kbd>` text — "?" and "Esc" render at different x positions (measured x=577/x=593), worsening the moment a third row lands. Replaced the per-row `.shortcuts-overlay__row` flex wrapper with a shared `.shortcuts-overlay__list` grid (`grid-template-columns: auto 1fr`, the same pattern as `.ticket-drawer__contract-grid`) — `<dt>`/`<dd>` are now direct grid children via a keyed `Fragment` per row (the pattern already used in `layout/SplitPaneWorkspace.tsx`, not a `display: contents` hack), so column alignment is structural, not row-local.

`trace.css` had no `.trace-event__type` rule, so the raw dotted event id inherited full `--sw-fg` at base size while its siblings (`__pass`, `__actor`, `__time`) were already muted — the least human-meaningful token was the loudest thing in the row. `.trace-event__type` now joins the same muted selector list. Timestamps rendered raw ISO-8601 UTC with milliseconds, unlocalised, with no step delta; `TraceView.tsx` now renders via `toLocaleString()` plus a `+Xms`/`+X.Ys`/`+XmYYs` delta since the previous row's timestamp — the fixture's ~1ms spacing is real replay data, left alone rather than "fixed."

AC4 (presets fold): apps/web has no `@dokima/gateway` dependency path (ARCHITECTURE §4), so `settings/types.ts`'s `MODEL_MATRIX_PRESETS` stays a hand-mirror by construction — W10-42 already pinned it server-side (`matrix-routes.test.ts`); `providers-api.test.ts` adds the same pin at the web layer via a dynamic `file://` import of `routing/presets.ts` (repo precedent: `e2e/fixtures/seed-board-tickets.mjs`), so it can no longer drift silently. `e2e/settings.spec.ts`'s "model matrix" test — the thing that sank attempt 2 — now registers a real `ollama` provider on a closed port (falls back to the bundled offline catalog, never live network, Law 9) and drives the select via `selectOption()`; the copilot/-backed-flag scenario is seeded through the real model-matrix PUT directly (an upsert, PK role+task_type, so it coexists with the UI-added row) since Copilot's own catalog isn't discoverable yet (UX_SPEC §6a "Cloud kind selected"). `e2e/a11y/settings.spec.ts` gained a second scan of the populated state (provider registered, matrix row picked) alongside the existing empty-state scan, since the empty scan alone never renders the new Providers table or a populated `<select>`, plus a third test proving AC5's "keyboard-operable end to end" clause with real `Tab` traversal (never `.focus()`) to the Providers row action and the Model `<select>`, operated by key press / `selectOption()` rather than `.click()` (same technique as `keyboard-only.spec.ts`'s verb-menu test) — an axe scan alone proves WCAG rules, not tab order. `validate-file-size.sh` flagged `ProvidersPanel.tsx` at 403 lines (> the 400 cap) on first pass; `write_scope` is exact file paths here, not a glob, so a `CODE_BOOK_PROTOCOL.md` directory split wasn't available — moved the three pure helpers (`KIND_LABEL`, `reachability()`, `removalCopy()`) into `providers-api.ts` instead (280→320 lines, still under the 300 warn), bringing the component to 366 lines with new direct unit tests for both moved functions. 2026-08-03 W10-27 done — the durable half of the 2026-08-02 release blocker: history scanning joined the gate. `content/validators/secrets-scan.sh` (W3-13, SC-06) greps the working **tree** and passes `--exclude-dir=.git`, so by construction it can never see a credential that was committed and later deleted — which is precisely how the Ed25519 content-signing private key sat in pushed history from 2026-07-20 to 2026-08-02 with every gate green the entire time. New `scripts/validate-history-secrets.mjs` reads every blob reachable from `git rev-list --objects --all`, using the same six categories as the tree scanner and the same masking rule (a matched value is never printed — `ghp_...REDACTED(n chars)` — because a scanner must not become the leak it exists to prevent, Law 8). **No external scanning binary**: git plumbing plus Node, against the W10_PLAN spec's assumed gitleaks dependency — measured at ~1.1s over 1064 commits / 3952 reachable blobs / 295 MB, so the dependency bought nothing and would have cost a prerequisite that is absent on most machines (Law 9); binary blobs are skipped by git's own NUL-in-first-8000-bytes heuristic, the `I` in the tree scanner's `grep -rEIn`. Exit `0` clean / `1` findings / `2` could-not-scan, the validator-pack contract. **BASELINE KEYS ON THE VALUE, NOT THE PATH** — `scripts/history-secrets-baseline.json` matches on `category + sha256(value)[0:16]` alone, because a path-scoped allowlist would let a real credential hide inside an already-baselined fixture file and would churn on every edit to one (blob shas change; values don't). 24 entries, each reviewed by hand rather than bulk-accepted: 21 test fixtures, and 3 documentation examples that are AWS's own published `AKIAIOSFODNN7EXAMPLE`, a `postgres://admin:hunter2@` doc sample, and a genuine false positive on the prose string `sk-at-most-one-question` in `ux-engineer.md`. **The scan also independently confirms the 2026-08-02 purge worked**: no PEM private key under `.keys/` appears anywhere in reachable history. **FAILS CLOSED**: `actions/checkout@v4` defaults to `fetch-depth: 1`, and with no history the scan would pass vacuously — a gate that cannot fail — so the CI job sets `fetch-depth: 0` **and** the script independently refuses (exit 2, never 0) on a shallow clone, an empty repo, a non-repo, or an unparseable baseline. RED FIXTURES (`scripts/validate-history-secrets.test.mjs`, 13 tests): a PEM key is committed, then deleted from the tree and gitignored — the exact 2026-08-02 shape — and `secrets-scan.sh`, **first proven to catch that same key while it is still in the tree** (the control that makes its later silence mean *blind* rather than *broken invocation*), then reads clean while this scanner exits 1 and hands over `git log --find-object=<blob>`, verified to resolve to the planting commit; a second unbaselined token added to an already-baselined fixture file still fails; neither stream ever contains a planted value. Fixture secrets in that file are assembled at runtime rather than written as literals — a literal would be committed once and then gate this repo forever, forcing a permanent baseline entry for a test's own fixture (a deliberate divergence from the older `secrets-scan-validator.test.ts`, whose literals are in today's baseline). Four planted defects against the scanner itself — shallow check disabled, first-match-only instead of `matchAll`, raw value instead of masked, unreadable baseline reading as clean — were each confirmed to turn the suite red before the fix was restored. Deliberately NOT added to `DEFAULT_REQUIRED_VALIDATORS`: the close gate's `classifySecretsGaps` calibration filters gaps to session-changed paths (D-014/L-28), which is right for a tree scan and actively wrong for history findings that are commit-scoped — that seam needs its own ticket, not a widened `write_scope` here. `docs/TESTING.md` §6a documents the tier and the four properties; `docs/RELEASE_TRACKER.md`'s pre-public checklist records that re-running this by hand before a tag is now a confirmation, not the control. Gate: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning in `docs/dogfood/run-dogfood.mjs`, unrelated), typecheck clean across 14 workspace projects, **415 files / 3102 passed | 3 skipped** (+1 file / +13 tests net), **61 e2e passed**; `validate-plan.mjs` / `validate-traceability.mjs` both clean.
2026-08-03 (2nd) W10-27 follow-up — closed a hole in my own deliverable, found by writing the commit message for the first one. The scanner enumerated `git rev-list --objects --all` and then filtered to `type === 'blob'`, so **commit messages were never scanned**. `git rev-list --objects` prints commit objects with *no path*, so the parser's `if (sp > 0)` guard silently discarded every one of them — the same class of defect as the ticket it was fixing: a scanner that looks confident while structurally blind to a whole category of history. A credential pasted into a commit message is published exactly like one in a file, and no tree edit removes it. `reachableBlobs` became `reachableObjects`: commits are kept (path `null`), annotated tags are added from `git for-each-ref refs/tags` (rev-list does not enumerate them), and the type filter now admits `blob`/`commit`/`tag`; findings report `commit message <sha7>` with a `git cat-file -p` hint instead of the blob-only `--find-object`. Object count over this repo went 3960 → 5025 (+1065 commit messages), still 0 gating, runtime ~1.3s. Two new red fixtures — a token in a commit message and one in an annotated tag message, each with a spotless working tree — and reverting the filter to `type === 'blob'` was confirmed to red exactly those two. Docs corrected in `TESTING.md` §1/§6a and `RELEASE_TRACKER.md`, which had all inherited the "every blob" phrasing. Gate: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning, unrelated), typecheck clean, **415 files / 3104 passed | 3 skipped** (+2 tests), **61 e2e passed**.
2026-08-03 (3rd) W10-27 follow-up — the CI job was trusting `fetch-depth: 0` to hand it a complete history, and that trust was unverified. Simulated both refspecs against a bare origin carrying a leaked `sk-` token on a second branch: with `+refs/heads/main:refs/remotes/origin/main` the scanner printed **"OK: no un-baselined credential shapes in git history"** over a repo that demonstrably has one. It is not shallow, so the shallowness check never fires — the denominator is just silently smaller, which is the precise failure mode this gate exists to kill, reproduced inside the gate itself. Fixed at both ends rather than one: the CI job now runs an explicit `git fetch --prune --no-tags origin '+refs/heads/*:refs/remotes/origin/*'` before scanning (so coverage does not depend on what `actions/checkout` happens to fetch), **and** the scanner refuses to trust that it did — `--verify-remote-refs` compares `git ls-remote --heads` against the local ref set and exits 2 naming any branch it cannot see. That flag is opt-in precisely because it is the one network call in the file: CI has already talked to the remote it cloned from, while a local run stays offline (Law 9). Output now reports the ref count as well as the object count, so a narrowed denominator is visible in every log. RED FIXTURE (16 tests total): the narrow clone is built for real, the blind `OK` is asserted as the defect, `--verify-remote-refs` is asserted to exit 2 naming `feature`, and the token is then found once the missing branch is fetched — all three states in one test, so it cannot pass by accident. Two limits now stated in `TESTING.md` §6a and `RELEASE_TRACKER.md` rather than left implied: the six categories are shared with the tree scanner, so an unpatterned credential shape is invisible to both; and `--all` is reachable-only, so a force-pushed and garbage-collected secret reads clean locally while it may still be retrievable from the forge's dangling objects — the same caveat the 2026-08-02 write-up records about what a rewrite does not undo. Gate: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning, unrelated), typecheck clean, **415 files / 3105 passed | 3 skipped** (+1 test), **61 e2e passed**.
2026-08-03 W10-43 done — the published tarball could not find its own assets. Claiming `@bpmforge/dokima` on npm (founder-authorized; 0.1.0 chosen by the founder) required scoping the root package name, and `packages/shared/src/paths/index.ts:31` identified the distribution root by matching `package.json` `name === 'dokima'` exactly. Scoping the name made `distributionRoot()` throw `DistributionRootNotFoundError` at module-evaluation time, so `content/`, both migration sets and the built web dist all became unreachable: `npm install` succeeded and then `dokima` died on startup. Found by actually packing and installing the tarball into a clean project — `npm pack --dry-run` listed a perfectly correct 240-file, 928 kB tarball and told me nothing, because the file list was never the problem. **Fixed the marker, not the string**: a name allowlist would have fixed this one rename and re-armed the trap for the next, so the root is now identified by the manifest declaring the `dokima` **bin** — the thing that actually makes a manifest *the distribution*. Both the source monorepo root and the published package carry it; an `@dokima/*` workspace manifest does not (preserving the existing "walks PAST @dokima/* manifests" property), and neither does a consumer's own `package.json`, which is exactly what sits above a real install. `isRootManifest` is now exported, because the probe starts from `import.meta.url` and `DOKIMA_DIST_ROOT` short-circuits it — the rule was unreachable from a test, which is why a rename got as far as a real install before anything noticed. +6 tests; reverting to name-equality reds 5, including the two pre-existing `distributionRoot` tests whose own assertion had encoded the identical brittle coupling (`expect(manifest.name).toBe('dokima')`) and is now asserted on the bin marker. Publish metadata added to the root `package.json`: scoped name, `0.1.0`, `publishConfig.access: public` (scoped packages default to restricted and would otherwise fail or bill), description/license/repository/homepage/bugs/keywords, and `prepublishOnly` running the build so a tarball can never ship without `apps/server/dist/main.js` or `apps/web/dist` (both are `files` entries that npm omits silently when missing). `.gitignore` now excludes `*.tgz` so a pack artifact cannot be committed. **VERIFIED ON A REAL INSTALL, twice** — pack, install into a clean project outside this repo, run the binary: it boots (`dokima: listening at http://127.0.0.1:4733`), serves the built web dist, materializes `packs/` under a fresh `DOKIMA_HOME`, and answers `GET /api/v1/projects` → `200 {"projects":[]}` with a real bearer token. Confirmed still open and filed as W10-44: `dokima --help` boots the server rather than printing usage — re-confirmed against the packaged install, and it matters more once `npx` makes it a stranger's first command. Gate: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning, unrelated), typecheck clean across 14 workspace projects, **415 files / 3111 passed | 3 skipped** (+6 net), **61 e2e passed**; history-secrets scan clean.
2026-08-03 W10-44 done — `dokima --help` booted the server instead of printing usage. `runPackagedCli`'s final statement was an unconditional `return runServerBoot(io, deps)`, so every unrecognised argument — `--help`, `--version`, a typo, or a known command missing its subcommand — fell through to detecting/binding a port and opening a browser. Measured against the packaged install, not inferred from source: `dokima --help` printed `dokima is already running at http://127.0.0.1:4317 — opening the Canvas` and never exited. Harmless in a source checkout; it is the first command a stranger runs after `npx @bpmforge/dokima`, which is what made it worth a ticket now rather than staying a Known-gaps line. Argument handling now runs BEFORE dispatch and never falls through: `--help`/`-h` print a real usage block (commands + the four `DOKIMA_*` env vars) and exit 0; `--version`/`-V` print the version read from the distribution's own manifest via `resolveAsset` (so it works from both a source checkout and an installed package — W10-43's marker fix is what makes that resolvable) and exit 0; an unknown first token prints `unknown command '<x>'` plus usage to **stderr** and exits **2**, never 0, because a script must be able to tell a typo from a real run; and `packs`/`providers` without their required subcommand now refuse with their own usage line instead of silently booting. Bare `dokima` is untouched — this ticket adds an argument surface, it does not change the default. RED FIXTURES (+6 tests, 17 total in cli.test.ts): every one asserts an **absence** — `detectRunningCore`, `runBootSequence`, `openBrowser`, `buildApiServer`, `listenLocalhost` and `ensureAuthToken` all un-called — because the defect was never a wrong message but a fall-through, and a stdout-only assertion would have passed against the bug. Proven red by `git stash`-ing only `cli.ts` and re-running against the real pre-fix code: 6 failed / 11 passed, then 17/17 after restoring. Also discharged a stale HANDOFF in `main.ts` claiming "there is no compile step in this repo yet… a real publish channel needs a build step; unverifiable here, no live registry" — false since W9-13's `apps/server/build.mjs`, and disproven outright by W10-43 driving a real installed tarball. VERIFIED ON THE PACKAGED INSTALL: pack → install into a clean project → `dokima --help` prints usage and exits 0 instantly, `dokima --version` prints `0.1.0`, `dokima docter` prints the unknown-command error and exits 2. Gate: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning, unrelated), typecheck clean across 14 workspace projects, **415 files / 3117 passed | 3 skipped** (+6 net), **61 e2e passed**.
2026-08-03 W10-45 done — the onboard/analysis path still resolved its model from three env vars; W10-03 wired the twin call site and left this one behind. `onboard-dispatch-port.ts` returned `{DOKIMA_MODEL_BASE_URL ?? http://127.0.0.1:1234/v1, DOKIMA_MODEL_API_KEY, DOKIMA_MODEL_ID ?? local-model}`, never imported `model-resolution.js`, and built an unconditional `createOaiCompatProvider` — so on the one path the W8-01 dogfood actually runs, whatever provider and model the user picked in the Providers panel was silently ignored. **Resolution moved INSIDE the dispatch function, not at construction**: the matrix is keyed role × task_type and every onboard step dispatches a different specialist, so resolving once would have wired the seam and still routed every specialist through one role's entry — a quieter version of the same bug, and the obvious wrong fix. **Imported rather than reimplemented**: `resolveGatewayConfigForProject` and `providerForConfig` come from `gateway-model-port.ts` (both already exported), so this inherits the kind-aware adapter construction and the named `kind-not-constructible` refusal for cloud kinds instead of silently falling back to localhost — W10_PLAN §6a traces this entire unwired seam to ticket-scoped local reimplementation, so copying either would have reproduced the cause while fixing the symptom. `onboard-run.ts` stopped passing `?? resolveOnboardGatewayConfigFromEnv()` eagerly: supplying an env-derived config meant `createRealOnboardDispatch` always saw an explicit config and could never reach the registry at all — the env fallback beat the user's own selection, the same bug pointing the other way. Env survives as the documented override that deliberately LOSES to an explicit selection, and `resolveModelTarget` still falls back to it when the registry or matrix is empty (a normal first-run state, C-1, not an error) — which is what keeps the e2e fake-model gateway working. `resolveOnboardGatewayConfigFromEnv` stays exported (`pipeline/index.ts` re-exports it, outside write_scope). RED FIXTURES (+4 tests, 7 in the file): **two real HTTP gateways**, not mocks — register providers `alpha` and `beta`, point the matrix row for `security-auditor` at `beta/chosen-model`, and assert `providerB.requests` has the call and `providerA.requests` is empty, because which socket received the request is the only thing that distinguishes a wired seam from a plausible-looking one. A second fixture proves per-role resolution by driving two roles to two different providers in one run — it fails against the resolve-once-at-construction fix. Two regression guards (env fallback with nothing configured; explicit config still winning) deliberately pass in both directions. Proven red by `git stash`-ing only the two source files and re-running against real pre-fix code: 2 failed / 5 passed, then 7/7 restored. 2026-08-03 W10-47 done — split `packages/events/src/receipts.ts` 553 → **51** (pure re-export barrel) over `receipts/` chapters: mac 156, verify 148, mint 200, query 54, waiver-policy 44, types 38. Tree-shaped deps exactly as `docs/work/FILE_SIZE_DEBT.md` derived (types ← waiver-policy/mac ← mint/verify, query ← mint), no cycles. `receipts.ts` keeps its exact path so `index.ts` and every consumer import `./receipts.js` unchanged — zero call sites moved. **The safety net was built BEFORE any code moved**, because this is the one split whose failure mode cannot be undone: `computeReceiptMac` feeds ten fields in a fixed order through `JSON.stringify` + length-prefixing, and every receipt already minted into the append-only hash-chained log (C-6) carries a MAC over that exact byte sequence — an append-only log cannot be migrated and the original inputs are gone. `receipts-golden.fixture.json` freezes 7 input-tree hashes and 11 MACs generated from the **pre-split** implementation, covering precisely the cases a reordering would hide: `null` vs `''` (the reason each field is independently JSON-encoded), `phase: 0` vs `phase: null`, values swapped between two fields, multi-validator arrays, unicode paths/content, empty content, and the `'a'+'bc'` vs `'ab'+'c'` boundary-shift pair that only length-prefixing separates. **The mutation result is the finding worth keeping**: swapping just two fields in `computeReceiptMac` (`projectId` ↔ `phase`) leaves **all 15 existing `receipts.test.ts` tests green** — they mint and verify with the same code, so they agree with themselves whatever the bytes are — and is caught only by the golden fixture.
